\displaystyle C\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{2}t_{ij}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}\right]\right|\right]

(4.38)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{2}t_{ij}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}\right]\right|\right]

(4.39)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{2}t_{ij}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta}\right]\right|\right]

(4.40)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{2}t_{ij}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta}\right]\right|\right]

(4.41)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{4}t_{ij}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{2}}\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{2}}\right]

(4.42)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{4}t_{ij}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{2}}\right]

Of the terms above, (
4.37
), (
4.38
), (
4.39
) and (
4.40
) are handled similarly, so we just estimate (
4.37
). Analogously, we will then estimate (
4.41
) as (
4.42
) is similar.

We begin with (
4.37
). Fixing a small
\varepsilon_{1}>0
, we break the integral over
x
and
y
into four terms, depending on whether
|x-\gamma_{i}|
and
|y-\gamma_{j}|
are less or greater than
N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}
. That is, we expand the identity,

(4.43)

1=(\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}+\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|\geq N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}})(\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}+\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|\geq N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}

and estimate the contribution of each of the four resulting terms.
We begin with the case that
|x-\gamma_{i}|,|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}
. For this term, we use the fact that,

(4.44)

\sup_{s,t}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|Z_{i}-s+\mathrm{i}N\eta||Z_{j}-t+\mathrm{i}N\eta|}\right]\leq C\log(N)^{2}

by the estimates of the covariance and variance of
Z_{i},Z_{j}
of Proposition
4.3
. The same estimate holds for,

(4.45)

\sup_{s,t}\mathbb{E}[|Z_{i}-s+\mathrm{i}N\eta|^{-1}]+\mathbb{E}[|Z_{j}-t+\mathrm{i}N\eta|^{-1}]\leq C\log(N).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}|g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)|\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{2}t_{ij}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}\right]\right|\right]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}|g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)|\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\frac{N^{2\varepsilon}}{N^{2}t_{ij}}\frac{N^{2}}{\eta}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{N^{2\varepsilon+\delta_{2}}}{\eta}\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}|g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)|\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\frac{1}{|x-y|+N^{-1}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(4.46)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{N^{2\varepsilon+\delta_{2}+2\varepsilon_{1}}}{\eta}\int_{R}|g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)|\frac{1}{|x-y|+N^{-1}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

The first inequality bounds
|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{-2}\leq\eta^{-1}|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{-1}
and then uses (
4.44
) and (
4.45
).
In the second inequality we used the fact that

(4.47)

t_{ij}\geq N^{-\delta_{2}}|x-y|+N^{-1}

which holds for all
|x-\gamma_{i}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}
and
|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}
as long as
\varepsilon_{1}<\delta_{1}-\delta_{2}
, due the inequalities (
4.19
). The final inequality is from the fact that for fixed
x
and
y
, we have

(4.48)

\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\leq CN^{2\varepsilon_{1}}.

For the integral, we have that

(4.49)

\displaystyle\int_{R}|g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)|\frac{1}{|x-y|+N^{-1}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\leq\int_{R}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\frac{1}{|x-y|+N^{-1}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\leq\log(N)\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}.

We now consider the contribution when
|x-\gamma_{i}|>N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}
and
|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}
. For this term, we estimate,

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}|g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)|\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|>N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{2}t_{ij}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}\right]\right|\right]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}|g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)|\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|>N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}

(4.50)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{2}t_{ij}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}N^{-1}|^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\left|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta\right|}+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\left|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta\right|}\right]\right)\right]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y.

We will not use any cancellation between
\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta
and its expectation, so we just estimate the contribution of
\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}N^{-1}|^{-2}|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{-1}]
to the summation and integral, the term where the expectation hits each term separately being similar. For this term, we use

(4.51)

|g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)|\leq|g_{k}(x)|^{2}+|g_{k}(y)|^{2},

resulting in two integrals. For the second, we estimate,

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}|g_{k}(y)|^{2}\frac{1}{N^{2}t_{ij}}\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}N^{-1}|^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}\right|\right]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{|y|<5}|g_{k}(y)|^{2}\frac{1}{Nt_{ij}}\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{-1}]\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq CN^{\varepsilon}\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{|y|<5}|g_{k}(y)|^{2}\frac{1}{t_{ij}}\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\mathrm{d}y

(4.52)

\displaystyle\leq CN^{1+2\varepsilon+\delta_{2}}\sum_{j\in J_{\alpha}}\int_{|y|<5}|g_{k}(y)|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\mathrm{d}y\leq CN^{1+2\varepsilon+\varepsilon_{1}+\delta_{2}}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}

In the first inequality we did the integral over
x
which contributes a factor of
N
. The second inequality follows from estimating the expectation using (
4.45
). The third inequality uses the fact that for all
j\in J_{\alpha}
,

(4.53)

\sum_{i\in J_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\frac{1}{t_{ij}}\leq N^{1+\varepsilon+\delta_{2}}.

The last inequality uses that, for all
y
,

(4.54)

\sum_{j\in J_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\leq CN^{\varepsilon_{1}}.

The other contribution to (
4.50
) after the Schwarz inequality (
4.51
) is bounded above by

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\frac{1}{N^{2}t_{ij}}\bm{1}_{\{|y-\gamma_{j}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}N^{-1}|^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}\right|\right]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{|x|<5}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{2}t_{ij}}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{i}-x)^{2}+N^{-2}}\mathrm{d}x

(4.55)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{|x|<5}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\sum_{i\in J_{\alpha}}\frac{N^{2\varepsilon+\delta_{2}}}{N}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{i}-x)^{2}+N^{-2}}\mathrm{d}x\leq CN^{2\varepsilon+\delta_{2}}N\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}.

In the first inequality we did the integral over
y
which contributes a factor of
\log(N)
at worst, and then used rigidity to estimate
|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}N^{-1}|\geq N^{-\varepsilon}|\gamma_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}N^{-1}|
with overwhelming probability. In the second inequality we used again (
4.53
) but for sums over
j
. The final inequality follows from the fact that,

(4.56)

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}}\frac{1}{N}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{i}-x)^{2}+N^{-2}}\leq CN,

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

Returning to (
4.37
) we now turn to the case
|x-\gamma_{i}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}
and
|y-\gamma_{j}|>N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}
. For such
y
, we see by rigidity that, with overwhelming probability

(4.57)

\left|\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}\right]\right|\leq\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{j}-y)^{2}+N^{-2}}.

So, we are led to estimate,

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}|g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)|\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{3}t_{ij}}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{j}-y)^{2}+N^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{-2}]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}|g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)|\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\frac{N^{2\varepsilon}}{N^{2}\eta t_{ij}}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{j}-y)^{2}+N^{-2}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

where we used again (
4.45
). To bound the last line of (
4
), we apply again the Schwarz inequality (
4.51
) and treat the two integrals separately. We first have,

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\frac{N^{2\varepsilon}}{N^{2}\eta t_{ij}}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{j}-y)^{2}+N^{-2}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{|x|<5}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\frac{N^{2\varepsilon}}{N\eta t_{ij}}\mathrm{d}x

(4.59)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{N^{3\varepsilon+\delta_{2}}}{\eta}\sum_{i\in J_{\alpha}}\int_{|x|<5}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{N^{3\varepsilon+\varepsilon_{1}+\delta_{2}}}{\eta}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}.

In the first inequality we did the
y
-integration, which contributes a factor of
N
. In the second inequality we did the sum over
j
and used (
4.53
), and used finally (
4.54
) in the third inequality. The other contribution to the last line of (
4
) is,

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}|g_{k}(y)|^{2}\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|<N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\frac{N^{2\varepsilon}}{N^{2}\eta t_{ij}}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{j}-y)^{2}+N^{-2}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{|y|<5}|g_{k}(y)|^{2}\frac{N^{2\varepsilon+\varepsilon_{1}}}{N^{3}\eta t_{ij}}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{j}-y)^{2}+N^{-2}}\mathrm{d}y

(4.60)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in J_{\alpha}}\int_{|y|<5}|g_{k}(y)|^{2}\frac{N^{3\varepsilon+\varepsilon_{1}+\delta_{2}}}{N^{2}\eta}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{j}-y)^{2}+N^{-2}}\mathrm{d}y\leq C\frac{N^{3\varepsilon+\varepsilon_{1}+\delta_{2}}}{\eta}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}.

In the first inequality we did the integration over
x
which is restricted to an interval of size
\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1})
. In the second inequality we did the summation over
i
using (
4.53
). The final inequality uses (
4.56
).

Finally, if
|x-\gamma_{i}|>N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}
and
|y-\gamma_{j}|>N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}
then,

\displaystyle\bm{1}_{\{|x-\gamma_{i}|>N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\bm{1}_{\{|\gamma_{j}-y|>N^{\varepsilon_{1}-1}\}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta}\right]\right|\right]

(4.61)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{i}-x)^{2}+N^{-2}}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{j}-y)^{2}+N^{-2}},

by rigidity.
Now,

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}\frac{1}{N^{3}t_{ij}}|g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)|\frac{1}{(\gamma_{i}-x)^{2}+N^{-2}}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{j}-y)^{2}+N^{-2}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}\frac{1}{N^{3}t_{ij}}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{i}-x)^{2}+N^{-2}}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{j}-y)^{2}+N^{-2}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,j)\in J^{2}_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{|x|<5}\frac{1}{N^{2}t_{ij}}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{i}-x)^{2}+N^{-2}}\mathrm{d}x

(4.62)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon+\delta_{2}}\sum_{i\in J_{\alpha}}\int_{|x|<5}\frac{1}{N}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\frac{1}{(\gamma_{i}-x)^{2}+N^{-2}}\mathrm{d}x\leq N^{1+\varepsilon+\delta_{2}}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}.

In the first inequality we applied (
4.51
). In the second we did the integration over
y
. In the third we used (
4.53
) to do the summation over
j
. In the last inequality we then used (
4.56
). This completes the estimation of (
4.37
). It remains to consider (
4.41
). We have,

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in J_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}\frac{1}{N^{4}t^{2}_{ij}}|g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)|\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{-2}|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{-2}]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in J_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{R}\frac{1}{N^{4}t^{2}_{ij}}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{-2}|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{-2}]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in J_{\alpha}}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{|x|<5}\frac{1}{N^{4}t^{2}_{ij}\eta}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{-2}]\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{2\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}}\sum_{i\in J_{\alpha}}\int_{|x|<5}\frac{1}{N}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{-2}]\mathrm{d}x

(4.63)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{2\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}}\sum_{i\in J_{\alpha}}\int_{|x|<5}\frac{1}{N\eta}|g_{k}(x)|^{2}\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{-1}]\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{CN^{\varepsilon+2\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}}}{\eta}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}

In the first inequality, we used (
4.51
). In the second inequality, we did the integration over
y
. In the third inequality, we used

(4.64)

\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{j}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\frac{1}{t_{ij}^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{N}\sum_{j}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\frac{N^{2\delta_{2}}}{|i-j|^{2}}\leq CN^{2\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}}\eta.

In the last inequality we used (
4.45
). This completes the proof. ∎

Proposition 4.5
.

Let
\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3}
be as above. Then, for
B_{1}>0
large enough, depending on
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}
and
\delta_{3}
we have for

(4.65)

10\log_{2}(N)\geq k\geq\log_{2}(B_{1}N)

that,

(4.66)

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)\mathrm{Cov}\big(\mathrm{Im}[(\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta)^{-1}],\mathrm{Im}[(\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta)^{-1})]\big)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|\leq CN^{-100}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}

Proof.
By Fourier duality and the independence of
Z_{i},Z_{j}
from
\hat{\mu}^{(ij)}_{i},\hat{\mu}^{(ij)}_{j},Y_{i},Y_{j}
(see Proposition
4.3
) we have,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)\mathbb{E}\big[\mathrm{Im}[(\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta)^{-1}]\mathrm{Im}[(\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta)^{-1}]\big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}_{k}(\xi_{1})\hat{\varphi}_{k}(\xi_{2})\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\xi_{1}(N^{-1}Y_{i}-\hat{\mu}^{(ij)}_{i})+\mathrm{i}\xi_{2}(N^{-1}Y_{j}-\hat{\mu}^{(ij)}_{j})\right)\right]

(4.67)

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\xi_{1}N^{-1}Z_{i}+\mathrm{i}\xi_{2}N^{-1}Z_{j}\right)\right]\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}.

By Proposition
4.3
we have,

(4.68)

\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\xi_{1}N^{-1}Z_{i}+\mathrm{i}\xi_{2}N^{-1}Z_{j}\right)\right]\right|\leq\exp\left[-c(\delta_{3}-\delta_{2})\log(N)N^{-2}(\xi_{1}^{2}+\xi_{2}^{2})\right]

By definition,
\hat{\varphi}_{k}(\xi)
is non-zero only if
|\xi|\geq B_{1}N/10
. By taking
B_{1}
large enough we see that,

(4.69)

\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\xi_{1}N^{-1}Z_{i}+\mathrm{i}\xi_{2}N^{-1}Z_{j}\right)\right]\right|\leq N^{-10^{7}}

for all such
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}
. Using this and the fact that

(4.70)

\|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\|_{1}\leq CN^{10}\|\hat{\varphi}_{k}\|_{2}=CN^{10}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}

by the fact that
\hat{\varphi}_{k}
is supported in a set of size at most
\mathcal{O}(N^{10})
, we conclude that

(4.71)

\left|\mathbb{E}[\varphi_{k}(\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i})\varphi_{k}(\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j})]\right|\leq N^{-100}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}.

The estimate for

(4.72)

\mathbb{E}[\varphi_{k}(\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i})]\mathbb{E}[\varphi_{k}(\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j})]

follows from similar reasoning. ∎

4.1
Proof of Theorem
3.4

Let
\omega>0
and
\delta>0
be as in the statement of the theorem. Let
\varepsilon>0
and assume
100\varepsilon<\delta-\omega
. Let
\delta_{1}=\delta
. Let
\kappa>0
so that
\varphi
is supported in
[-2+\kappa,2-\kappa]
and choose
\alpha>0
such that
\gamma_{\alpha N}\leq-2+\kappa/10
. For
k
that satisfy,

(4.73)

(2-\delta_{1}+\varepsilon)\log_{2}(N)\leq k\leq A\log_{2}(N)

we see that
\eta=2^{-k}
satisfies,

(4.74)

\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\geq N^{1+\varepsilon}\gg 1.

Therefore, by Propositions
4.1
and
4.2
, we have for

(4.75)

(2-\delta_{1}+\varepsilon)\log_{2}(N)\leq k\leq 2\log_{2}(N)

that,

(4.76)

\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}(\varphi_{k}(H)))\leq CN^{\delta+\varepsilon}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}2^{k}+C2^{-Mk}\|\varphi\|_{2}^{2}

For

(4.77)

2\log_{2}(N)\leq k\leq A\log_{2}(N),

we can apply Propositions
4.1
and
4.2
with
\delta_{1}=\varepsilon
to conclude that,

(4.78)

\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}(\varphi_{k}(H)))\leq CN^{2\varepsilon}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}2^{k}+C2^{-Mk}\|\varphi\|_{2}^{2}.

Let
\delta_{2}
and
\delta_{3}
satisfy
\omega<\delta_{3}<\delta_{2}<\delta_{1}
and be equally spaced between
\omega
and
\delta_{1}
. By Propositions
4.1
and
4.4
we see that for
k
satisfying,

(4.79)

\log_{2}(N)\leq k\leq(2-\delta_{1}+\varepsilon)\log_{2}(N)

we have,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}\varphi_{k}(H)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\bm{1}_{\{|i-j|>\frac{N^{\delta_{1}}}{N\eta}\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g_{k}(x)g_{k}(y)\mathrm{Cov}(\mathrm{Im}[(\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta)^{-1}],\mathrm{Im}[(\hat{\lambda}^{(ij)}_{j}-y-\mathrm{i}\eta)^{-1}])\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(4.80)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(N^{\delta+\varepsilon}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}2^{k}+N^{2}2^{-Mk}\|\varphi\|_{2}^{2}).

Due to the lower bound
k\geq\log_{2}(N)
we may absorb the
N^{2}
into the
2^{-Mk}
.
Now, the first term on the right side is estimated by Proposition
4.5
, after taking
B_{1}
large enough, which completes the proof. ∎

5
Micro and mesoscopic frequencies

Let
H
be a Wigner matrix.
Let
\mathfrak{s}>0
. We consider
\varphi\in H^{1/2+\mathfrak{s}}
. Recall the definition of
\varphi_{k}
and
g_{k}
as in Section
2.3
. In this section, we fix a small
\frac{1}{100}>\mathfrak{b}>0
and consider
k
in the range

(5.1)

\mathfrak{b}\log_{2}(N)\leq k\leq\log_{2}(N\mathfrak{b}^{-1}).

Fixing a large
A_{1}>0
, we will also introduce the following,

(5.2)

\hat{f}_{k}(\xi):=\mathrm{e}^{A_{1}N^{-2}\xi^{2}/2}\hat{g}_{k}(\xi).

We will see below that
A_{1}
is chosen large depending on the fourth cumulant
s_{4}
. We first show the following.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
1\leq p\leq\infty
. Let
0\leq k\leq\log_{2}(N\mathfrak{b}^{-1})
. There is a
C>0
(depending on
A_{1}
and
\mathfrak{b}
) so that,

(5.3)

\|f_{k}\|_{p}\leq C\|\varphi_{k}\|_{p}

for all such
k
.

Proof.
This is similar to the proof of Theorem
2.8
. Let
\hat{\psi}
be a bump function adapted to a ball of radius
R
, with
R
chosen large enough depending on
\mathfrak{b}
so that

(5.4)

\hat{\psi}(\xi/N)\hat{g}_{k}(\xi)=\hat{g}_{k}(\xi)

for all
\xi
and
k\leq\log_{2}(N\mathfrak{b}^{-1})
. Then, if
h
is the inverse Fourier transform of the function,

(5.5)

\hat{h}(\xi)=\hat{\psi}(\xi/N)\mathrm{e}^{A_{1}\xi^{2}/(2N^{2})},

the norm
\|h\|_{1}
is a constant not depending
N
. Since

(5.6)

f_{k}=h\star g_{k}

we see that
\|h_{k}\|_{p}\leq\|h\|_{1}\|g_{k}\|_{p}
by Young’s inequality. On the other hand, for
k\geq 0
it was shown in the proof of Theorem
2.8
(i.e.,
[
55
, Theorem 5]
) that,

(5.7)

\|g_{k}\|_{p}\leq C\|\varphi_{k}\|_{p}

for all
k\geq 0
. The claim follows. ∎

In a similar way to Proposition
2.9
we have the following.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\varphi\in H^{s}
or
C^{\alpha}
, and let
k
satisfy

(5.8)

\mathfrak{b}\log_{2}(N)\leq k\leq\log_{2}(N\mathfrak{b}^{-1})

Let
\kappa>0
and assume that
\varphi
is supported in
(-2+\kappa,2-\kappa)
. Let
D>0
. There is a constant
C>0
so that for any
x\notin(-2+\kappa/2,2-\kappa/2)
and
k
as above we have

(5.9)

|f_{k}(x)|\leq\frac{CN^{-D}}{1+|x|^{D}}\|\varphi\|_{2}.

Now, let
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
be independent centered Gaussians with mean
0
and variance
A_{1}/N^{2}
. We use the notation
\tilde{Z}=(Z_{1},Z_{2})^{T}
. Our starting point is the following representation for the variance associated to
\varphi_{k}
. Obviously,

(5.10)

\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}\varphi_{k}(H))\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathrm{tr}\varphi_{k}(H)-N\int\varphi_{k}(x)\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\mathrm{d}x\right)^{2}\right],

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathrm{tr}\varphi_{k}(H)-N\int\varphi_{k}(x)\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\mathrm{d}x\right)^{2}\right]

(5.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f_{k}(x)f_{k}(y)N^{2}\mathbb{E}^{(\tilde{Z})}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(x+Z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(y+Z_{2}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y,

where the outer expectation is with respect to
\tilde{Z}
and the inner expectation is conditional on
\tilde{Z}
. The role of
\tilde{Z}
will only become apparent later. For now, we note that due to the Gaussian decay of
\tilde{Z}
and Proposition
5.2
, we can restrict our attention to the case that
x+Z_{1}
and
y+Z_{2}
are well-separated from the edges of the semicircle distribution.

We now turn to the application of Theorem
2.6
. We now fix
\omega>0
and let

(5.12)

T=N^{-\omega}.

The results of this section will be applied with
\omega>0
small, depending on the regularity of
\varphi
that is quantified in terms of the parameter
\mathfrak{s}>0
above. We consider Gaussian divisible ensembles,

(5.13)

H_{T}=\mathrm{e}^{-T/2}W+\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-T}}G.

where
W
is a Wigner matrix.
We consider the eigenvalues of
H_{T}
to be those of a DBM process as in (
2.21
) which we denote by
\{\lambda_{i}(t)\}_{i=1}^{N}
,
0\leq t\leq T
. We couple this to an equilibrium process
\{\mu_{i}(t)\}_{i=1}^{N}
as described in Section
2.2
.

Define
J_{\alpha}
as in (
4.4
), and for
\kappa>0
we consider
\alpha>0
so that
\gamma_{\alpha N}<-2+\kappa/10
.
Fixing
x\in(-2+\kappa,2-\kappa)
, we consider the estimate

(5.14)

|\lambda_{i}(T)-\hat{\mu}_{i}(T,x)-N^{-1}Y(x)|\leq N^{\varepsilon}\frac{N|x-\gamma_{i}|+1}{N^{2}T}

which, thanks to Theorem
2.6
, holds with overwhelming probability for
i\in J_{\alpha}
, where we defined

(5.15)

Y(x):=X^{W}(x,T)

and

(5.16)

\hat{\mu}_{i}(T,x):=\mu_{i}(T)-N^{-1}X^{G}(x,T),

with the
X
’s defined in (
2.26
) and (
2.27
). Note that the random variable
X^{W}(x,T)
is independent of the
\hat{\mu}_{i}
’s, and that by rigidity,

(5.17)

|N^{-1}Y(x)|+|N^{-1}X^{G}(x,T)|\leq N^{\varepsilon-1}

with overwhelming probability, for any
\varepsilon>0
.
We also have the analogous estimates to all of those above with
x
replaced by
y
. We now use the estimate (
5.14
) to prove the following.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
\varepsilon_{1}>0
and
\varepsilon>0
. Fix
x,y\in(-2+\kappa,2-\kappa)
. Let
\hat{x}
and
\hat{y}
satisfy

(5.18)

|x-\hat{x}|+|y-\hat{y}|\leq\frac{N^{\varepsilon_{1}/10}}{N}.

Then,

\displaystyle N^{2}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(\hat{x}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(\hat{y}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sum_{i}\mathrm{Im}[(\hat{\mu}_{i}(x)-N^{-1}Y(x)-\hat{x}-\mathrm{i}\eta)^{-1}-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\hat{x}+N^{-1}Y(x)+\mathrm{i}\eta)]

\displaystyle\times\sum_{i}\mathrm{Im}[(\hat{\mu}_{i}(y)-N^{-1}Y(y)-\hat{y}-\mathrm{i}\eta)^{-1}-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\hat{y}+N^{-1}Y(y)+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\bigg\}

(5.19)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\eta^{-1}N^{\varepsilon_{1}+\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}\left(1+T^{-1}\right).

Proof.
The estimate (
5.14
) implies,

(5.20)

\left|\frac{1}{\lambda_{i}(T)-\hat{x}-\mathrm{i}\eta}-\frac{1}{\hat{\mu}_{i}(T,x)-\hat{x}-N^{-1}Y(x)-\mathrm{i}\eta}\right|\leq N^{\varepsilon_{1}+\varepsilon}\frac{N|x-\gamma_{i}|+1}{N^{2}T}\frac{1}{|\gamma_{i}-x-\mathrm{i}\eta|^{2}},

with overwhelming probability for
i\in J_{\alpha}
. For
i\notin J_{\alpha}
we have that

(5.21)

|\lambda_{i}(T)-\gamma_{i}|+|\gamma_{i}-(\mu_{i}(T,x)-N^{-1}Y(x))|\leq\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{2/3}\min\{i^{1/3},(N+1-i)^{1/3}\}}

with overwhelming probability, which implies, since
\gamma_{i}
is well-separated from
\hat{x}
that

(5.22)

\left|\frac{1}{\lambda_{i}(T)-\hat{x}-\mathrm{i}\eta}-\frac{1}{\hat{\mu}_{i}(T,x)-\hat{x}-N^{-1}Y(x)-\mathrm{i}\eta}\right|\leq\frac{CN^{\varepsilon}}{N^{2/3}\min\{i^{1/3},(N+1-i)^{1/3}\}}

Summing over
i
we see that with overwhelming probability that,

(5.23)

\displaystyle\left|N\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(\hat{x}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]-\sum_{i}\mathrm{Im}(\hat{\mu}_{i}(T,x)-\hat{x}-N^{-1}Y(x)-\mathrm{i}\eta)^{-1}\right|\leq N^{\varepsilon_{1}+2\varepsilon}\frac{1}{T}+N^{\varepsilon}

By the smoothness of
m_{\mathrm{sc}}
we see that,

(5.24)

|m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\hat{x}+\mathrm{i}\eta)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\hat{x}+N^{-1}Y(x)+\mathrm{i}\eta)|\leq N^{\varepsilon-1},

with overwhelming probability.
Therefore, with overwhelming probability,

\displaystyle N\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(\hat{x}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]-N\mathrm{Im}[m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\hat{x}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\mathrm{Im}(\hat{\mu}_{i}(T,x)-\hat{x}-N^{-1}Y(x)-\mathrm{i}\eta)^{-1}-N\mathrm{Im}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\hat{x}+Y(x)+\mathrm{i}\eta)

(5.25)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon_{1}+2\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}\left(1+\frac{1}{T}\right).

By rigidity, the second last line of (
5.25
) is
N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(\eta^{-1})
with overwhelming probability. We also arrive at similar estimates changing
x
and
\hat{x}
for
y
and
\hat{y}
. The claim then follows by expanding out the expectation in terms of the quantities on the second last line of (
5.25
). ∎

Eventually we are going to use the estimates of Theorem
3.2
to replace the appearance of
Y(x)
and
Y(y)
in the estimates above by explicit distributions. In order to do this, we first require some estimates that will be useful when we apply Fourier duality. For this, let
\chi
be a smooth bump function that is
1
on
[-1,1]
and
0
outside of
[-2,2]
. Fix now an

(5.26)

\varepsilon_{2}>0

and let

(5.27)

\rho_{2}(x)=\chi\big(x/(N^{\varepsilon_{2}}+(N\eta))\big)

and consider the functions, defined for
E\in\mathbb{R}
by,

(5.28)

F_{1,\hat{x},x}(E):=\sum_{i}\mathrm{Im}\left[(N(\hat{\mu}_{i}(T,x)-\hat{x})-E-\mathrm{i}N\eta)^{-1}\right]-\mathrm{Im}\left[m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\hat{x}+N^{-1}E+\mathrm{i}\eta)\right]

and

(5.29)

F_{2,\hat{x},x}(E):=\rho_{2}(E)F_{1,\hat{x},x}(E).

We have the following estimate for the Fourier transform of the (random) function
F_{2}
,

(5.30)

\hat{F}_{2,x,\hat{x}}(\xi)=\int\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}x\xi}F_{2,x,\hat{x}}(z)\mathrm{d}z.

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
\kappa>0
and let
|x|,|\hat{x}|\leq 2-\kappa
.
For any
k>0
and
\varepsilon>0
there is a constant
C
such that, with overwhelming probability,

(5.31)

|\hat{F}_{2,\hat{x},x}(\xi)|\leq CN^{\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon}\frac{1}{1+|\xi N\eta|^{k}}.

as well as,

(5.32)

|\hat{F}_{2,\hat{x},x}(\xi)|\leq CN^{\varepsilon+\varepsilon_{2}}.

Proof.
For the proof, denote

(5.33)

\hat{m}(w):=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\frac{1}{\hat{\mu}_{i}(T,x)-w},

so that

(5.34)

F_{1,\hat{x},x}(E)=\mathrm{Im}\left[\hat{m}(\hat{x}+\mathrm{i}\eta+EN^{-1})-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\hat{x}+\mathrm{i}\eta+EN^{-1})\right].

We have,

(5.35)

|\hat{\mu}_{i}(T,x)-\gamma_{i}|\leq\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{2/3}\min\{i^{1/3},(N+1-i)^{1/3}\}}

with overwhelming probability. Using these estimates, it is not hard to establish that

(5.36)

|\hat{m}(E+\mathrm{i}\eta)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(E+\mathrm{i}\eta)|\leq\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N\eta}

for any
E\in\mathbb{R}
with overwhelming probability. The second estimate of the Proposition now follows because the function
F_{2,\hat{x},x}
is supported in a set of size
\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon_{2}}+N\eta)
.

By the Cauchy integral formula and the estimates (
5.36
),

(5.37)

|\partial^{k}_{z}(\hat{m}(E+\mathrm{i}\eta)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(E+\mathrm{i}\eta))|\leq C_{k}\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N\eta^{k+1}}

with overwhelming probability. For holomorphic
f
we have by the Cauchy-Riemann equations,

(5.38)

\partial_{E}\mathrm{Im}[f]=\mathrm{Im}[f^{\prime}],

and so the estimate,

(5.39)

|\partial^{k}_{E}F_{1,\hat{x},x}(E)|\leq\frac{C_{k}}{(N\eta)^{k+1}}

holds. The first estimate now follows. ∎

For
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, define
H(z_{1},z_{2})
by,

(5.40)

H(z_{1},z_{2}):=\mathbb{E}^{(\mu)}\left[F_{2,x,x}(z_{1})F_{2,y,y}(z_{2})\right].

where the notation
\mathbb{E}^{(\mu)}
means the expectation over the
\hat{\mu}_{i}
that appear in the definition of the functions
F_{2}
above. The previous proposition implies the following Corollary for the behavior of the Fourier transform of
H
.

Corollary 5.5
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
D>0
. Then for
|\xi_{1}|+|\xi_{2}|\geq N^{\varepsilon}(N\eta)^{-1}
we have,

(5.41)

|\hat{H}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})|\leq CN^{-D}\frac{1}{1+|\xi_{1}|^{4}+|\xi_{2}|^{4}}.

Moreover, for any
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}
we have for any
\varepsilon>0
,

(5.42)

|\hat{H}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})|\leq CN^{\varepsilon+2\varepsilon_{2}}.

Proof.
On the event of
\mathcal{O}(N^{-D})
probability that the estimates of the previous Proposition do not hold, we have the deterministic estimate,

(5.43)

|\partial_{E}^{k}F_{2,x,x}(E)|\leq C_{k}\eta^{-1}(N\eta)^{-k}

for any
k\geq 0
and so we conclude the proof. ∎

Given
x
,
y
and
T>0
we now define a few matrices. Let
s_{3}
and
s_{4}
denote the third and fourth cumulants of the off-diagonal entries
\sqrt{N}W_{12}
of the Wigner matrix
W
in the definition (
5.13
) of
H_{T}
. For functions
\phi_{i}
, we define the quadratic forms

(5.44)

V_{1}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2}):=\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{-2}^{2}\frac{\left(\phi_{1}(x)-\phi_{1}(y)\right)\left(\phi_{2}(x)-\phi_{2}(y)\right)}{(x-y)^{2}}\frac{4-xy}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}\sqrt{4-y^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

and

\displaystyle V_{2}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{s_{4}}{2\pi^{2}}\left(\int_{-2}^{2}\phi_{1}(x)\frac{2-x^{2}}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\right)\left(\int_{-2}^{2}\phi_{2}(x)\frac{2-x^{2}}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{s_{3}}{\pi^{2}N^{1/2}}\left(\int_{-2}^{2}\phi_{1}(x)\frac{2-x^{2}}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\right)\left(\int_{-2}^{2}\phi_{2}(x)\frac{x}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\right)

(5.45)

\displaystyle-\frac{s_{3}}{\pi^{2}N^{1/2}}\left(\int_{-2}^{2}\phi_{2}(x)\frac{2-x^{2}}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\right)\left(\int_{-2}^{2}\phi_{1}(x)\frac{x}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\right)

and the third-order homogeneous polynomial

(5.46)

\displaystyle P_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=-\frac{s_{3}}{12\pi^{3}}\left(\xi_{1}\int_{-2}^{2}\phi_{1}(x)\frac{x}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x+\xi_{2}\int_{-2}^{2}\phi_{2}(x)\frac{x}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\right)^{3}

For
x\in(-2+\kappa,2-\kappa)
define,

(5.47)

\phi_{x}(E):=\frac{\mathrm{Im}[\log(E-x_{T})]}{\mathrm{Im}[m_{\mathrm{sc}}(x_{T})]}.

We form now the
2\times 2
matrices and
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
by,

(5.48)

M_{i}=\left(\begin{matrix}V_{i}(\phi_{x},\phi_{x})&V_{i}(\phi_{x},\phi_{y})\\
V_{i}(\phi_{x},\phi_{y})&V_{i}(\phi_{y},\phi_{y})\end{matrix}\right)

and the third-order homogeneous polynomial,

(5.49)

P(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})=P_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\phi_{x},\phi_{y}).

We define also,

(5.50)

e_{x}=e(\phi_{x}),\qquad e_{y}=e(\phi_{y}),\qquad e_{i,x}=e_{i}(\phi_{x}),\qquad e_{i,y}=e_{i}(\phi_{y}),

where the functionals
e(f)
and
e_{i}(f)
are as in (
3.5
), for
i=1,2
. Note that it is clear that,

(5.51)

|e_{i,x}|+|e_{i,y}|\leq C

for
i=1,2
.
We also denote,

(5.52)

\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

The following is an immediate consequence of Theorem
3.2
.

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. Then for
|\xi_{1}|+|\xi_{2}|\leq N^{1/10}
we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\xi_{1}(Y(x)-e_{x})+\mathrm{i}\xi_{2}(Y(y)-e_{y})\right)\right]-\exp\left(-\xi^{T}(M_{1}+M_{2})\xi+\mathrm{i}N^{-1/2}P(\xi)\right)\right|

(5.53)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{NT}(1+|\xi|^{7}).

This motivates the following definitions. Fix an
\varepsilon_{3}>0
satisfying

(5.54)

\frac{1}{100}>\varepsilon_{3}>0

and let,

(5.55)

\psi_{1}(\xi)=\exp\left(-\xi^{T}M_{1}\xi\right)

and

(5.56)

\psi_{2}(\xi)=\exp\left(-\xi^{T}M_{2}\xi-\xi_{1}^{2}A_{1}-\xi_{2}^{2}A_{1}+N^{-1/2}\mathrm{i}P(\xi)\right)\chi(\xi_{1}/N^{\varepsilon_{3}})\chi(\xi_{2}/N^{\varepsilon_{3}}).

Note that
\psi_{1}
is the “universal component” of the asymptotic joint distribution of
(Y(x),Y(y))
, and
\psi_{2}
is the non-universal component, which also includes our Gaussian convolution. Note also that the matrix
M_{1}
is positive semi-definite since,

(5.57)

\xi^{T}M_{1}\xi=V_{1}(\xi_{1}\phi_{x}+\xi_{2}\phi_{y},\xi_{1}\phi_{x}+\xi_{2}\phi_{y})\geq 0.

Whether or not
\psi_{1}
decays will not play any role due to the estimates we have derived for
\hat{H}
above.

We now choose
A_{1}
sufficiently large that,

(5.58)

|\xi^{T}M_{2}\xi^{T}+\xi_{1}^{2}A_{1}+\xi_{2}^{2}A_{1}-N^{-1/2}\mathrm{i}P(\xi)|\geq 10(\xi_{1}^{2}+\xi_{2}^{2})

for all
\xi
satisfying
|\xi_{1}|+|\xi_{2}|\leq N^{1/20}
. Note that this is possible since the entries of
M_{2}
are bounded independently of
T
and
|P(\xi)|\leq CN^{-1/4}
for such
\xi
.
Now, let
q(z_{1},z_{2})
be the inverse Fourier transform of
\psi_{2}
, that is

(5.59)

\psi_{2}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\xi_{1}z_{1}+\xi_{2}z_{2})}q(z_{1},z_{2})\mathrm{d}z_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{2}.

The function
q(z_{1},z_{2})
may not be a probability distribution, but it has sufficient properties suitable for our purposes, summarized in the following.

Proposition 5.7
.

The function
q(z_{1},z_{2})
is real-valued and satisfies,

(5.60)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}q(z_{1},z_{2})\mathrm{d}z_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{2}=1.

For any
k>0
, there is a
C>0
so that,

(5.61)

|q(z_{1},z_{2})|\leq C\frac{1}{1+|z_{1}|^{k}+|z_{2}|^{k}}.

Proof.
The function is real-valued since its Fourier transform satisfies
\bar{\psi}_{2}(\xi)=\psi_{2}(-\xi)
. The integral of
q
is
1
since
\psi_{2}(0,0)=1
. The decay estimates follow from the fact that the choices (
5.54
) and (
5.58
) implies that,

(5.62)

\|(\partial^{k}_{\xi_{1}}\partial^{l}_{\xi_{2}})\psi_{2}\|_{p}\leq C_{l,k,p}

for any
p,k,l
. ∎

Introduce also
p(z_{3},z_{4})
, a Gaussian distribution with covariance
M_{1}
, and define the function
F(z_{1},z_{2})
by

(5.63)

F(z_{1},z_{2}):=\int p(z_{3},z_{4})\mathbb{E}[F_{1,x,x}(z_{1}+z_{3}+e_{1,x})F_{1,y,y}(z_{2}+z_{4}+e_{1,y})]\mathrm{d}z_{3}\mathrm{d}z_{4}.

Eventually we will see that the function
F(z_{1},z_{2})
well-approximates the covariance of two resolvents of the GOE. First, we require the following elementary estimate on the distribution
p(z_{3},z_{4})
.

Lemma 5.8
.

For any
D>0
and
\varepsilon>0
there exists
C>0
so that,

(5.64)

\int_{|z_{3}|>N^{\varepsilon}}p(z_{3},z_{4})\mathrm{d}z_{3}\mathrm{d}z_{4}\leq CN^{-D}

and an analogous estimate for
z_{4}
.

Proof.
It suffices to find an upper bound on the diagonal entries of
M_{1}
. We note that for
|x|\leq 2-\kappa
that

(5.65)

V_{1}(\phi_{x},\phi_{x})\leq C\left(1+\int_{|u|,|v|\leq 2-\kappa/2}\left(\frac{\phi_{x}(u)-\phi_{x}(v)}{u-v}\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\right).

The integral on the RHS can be estimated via the same method as in the proof of Lemma 5.19 of
[
43
]
. This results in an upper bound of
\mathcal{O}(\log(N))
, which is sufficient for our purposes. ∎

The following result replaces the kernel appearing in the represenation (
5
) for the variance associated to
\varphi_{k}
with the function
F(z_{1},z_{2})
defined above. Later, the function
F
will be related to the covariance kernel of the GOE.

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
be given, and let
\varepsilon_{3}>0
and
\varepsilon_{2}>0
be as above. We have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{(\tilde{Z})}[N^{2}\mathbb{E}^{(H)}[\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(x+Z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(y+Z_{2}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int q(z_{1},z_{2})F(z_{1}+e_{2,x},z_{2}+e_{2,y})\mathrm{d}z_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{2}

(5.66)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon+2\varepsilon_{2}}\mathcal{O}(\eta^{-1}T^{-1})

Proof.
By applying Proposition
5.3
with
\hat{x}=x+Z_{1}
and
\hat{y}=y+Z_{2}
, we first see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{(\tilde{Z})}[N^{2}\mathbb{E}^{(H)}[\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(x+Z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(y+Z_{2}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]]]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{(\tilde{Z},Y)}N^{2}\mathbb{E}^{(\mu)}[F_{1,x,x}(NZ_{1}+Y(x))F_{1,y,y}(NZ_{2}+Y(y))]

(5.67)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(\eta^{-1}T^{-1}).

It is straightforward to conclude from the rigidity estimates that
|Y(x)|+|Y(y)|\leq N^{\varepsilon}
with overwhelming probability, for any
\varepsilon>0
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{(\tilde{Z},Y)}N^{2}\mathbb{E}^{(\mu)}[F_{1,x,x}(NZ_{1}+Y(x))F_{1,y,y}(NZ_{2}+Y(y))]

(5.68)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{(\tilde{Z},Y)}N^{2}\mathbb{E}^{(\mu)}[F_{2,x,x}(NZ_{1}+Y(x))F_{2,y,y}(NZ_{2}+Y(y))]+\mathcal{O}(N^{-100}).

Recall that
H(z_{1},z_{2})
is defined by

(5.69)

H(z_{1},z_{2})=\mathbb{E}^{(\mu)}[F_{2,x,x}(z_{1})F_{2,y,y}(z_{2})].

By Fourier duality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{(\tilde{Z},Y)}N^{2}\mathbb{E}^{(\mu)}[F_{2,x,x}(NZ_{1}+Y(x))F_{2,y,y}(NZ_{2}+Y(x))]

(5.70)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\hat{H}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\mathrm{e}^{-A_{1}(\xi_{1}^{2}+\xi_{2}^{2})}\psi(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\xi_{1}e_{x}+\mathrm{i}\xi_{2}e_{y}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2},

where we denoted

(5.71)

\psi(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\xi_{1}(Y(x)-e_{x})+\mathrm{i}\xi_{2}(Y(y)-e_{y})\right)\right].

By Proposition
5.6
and the estimates for
\hat{H}
from Corollary
5.5
we have,

\displaystyle N^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\hat{H}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\mathrm{e}^{-A_{1}(\xi_{1}^{2}+\xi_{2}^{2})}\psi(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\xi_{1}e_{x}+\mathrm{i}\xi_{2}e_{y}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\hat{H}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\psi_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\psi_{2}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\xi_{1}e_{x}+\mathrm{i}\xi_{2}e_{y}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}+N^{10\varepsilon+2\varepsilon_{2}}\mathcal{O}(\eta^{-2}(NT)^{-1})

(5.72)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}q(z_{1},z_{2})p(z_{3},z_{4})H(z_{1}+z_{3}+e_{x},z_{2}+z_{4}+e_{y})]\mathrm{d}z_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{2}\mathrm{d}z_{3}\mathrm{d}z_{4}+N^{10\varepsilon+2\varepsilon_{2}}\mathcal{O}(\eta^{-2}(NT)^{-1}),

where we used Fourier duality again in the last line. By the decay of
p
and
q
we see that,

\displaystyle N^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}q(z_{1},z_{2})p(z_{3},z_{4})H(z_{1}+z_{3}+e_{x},z_{2}+z_{4}+e_{y})]\mathrm{d}z_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{2}\mathrm{d}z_{3}\mathrm{d}z_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}q(z_{1},z_{2})p(z_{3},z_{4})\mathbb{E}^{(\mu)}[F_{1,x,x}(z_{1}+z_{3}+e_{x})F_{1,y,y}(z_{2}+z_{4}+e_{y})]\mathrm{d}z_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{2}\mathrm{d}z_{3}\mathrm{d}z_{4}

(5.73)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(N^{-100}).

But, by definition,

\displaystyle N^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}q(z_{1},z_{2})p(z_{3},z_{4})\mathbb{E}^{(\mu)}[F_{1,x,x}(z_{1}+z_{3}+e_{x})F_{1,y,y}(z_{2}+z_{4}+e_{y})]\mathrm{d}z_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{2}\mathrm{d}z_{3}\mathrm{d}z_{4}

(5.74)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}q(z_{1},z_{2})F(z_{1}+e_{2,x},z_{2}+e_{2,y})\mathrm{d}z_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{2}.

This yields the claim. ∎

Let
Y^{G^{\prime}}(x):=X^{G^{\prime}}(x,T)
where
G^{\prime}
is an auxilliary Gaussian matrix, coupled to the process
\mu_{i}(t)
in the same manner as
\lambda_{i}(t)
.

By Theorem
3.2
we have,

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
G^{\prime}
be a GOE matrix. Then,

(5.75)

\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\xi_{1}(Y(x)-e_{1,x})+\mathrm{i}\xi_{2}(Y(y)-e_{1,y})\right)\right]-\exp\left(-\xi^{T}M_{1}\xi\right)\right|\leq C\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{NT}(1+|\xi|^{7}).

Consider now repeating the above arguments with the matrix,

(5.76)

G_{t}:=\mathrm{e}^{-t/2}G^{\prime}+\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-t}}G

where
G^{\prime}
is an independent Gaussian matrix, in the place of
H_{t}
as defined in (
5.13
) (i.e., replace the matrix
W
there with a GOE matrix). In this case, we do not introduce the auxilliary variables
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
, and instead of introducing the function
\psi_{2}
, it is replaced by the constant function
\psi_{2}(\xi)=1
, and
q
is just the
\delta
function at
0
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Then, we see that the analog of Proposition
5.9
will be the following, obtained by almost exactly the same arguments.

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
G^{\prime}
be a Gaussian matrix. Let
\varepsilon>0
and
\varepsilon_{1}>0
. Assume that,

(5.77)

|z_{1}|+|z_{2}|\leq N^{\varepsilon_{1}/100}

Then,

\displaystyle N^{2}\mathbb{E}^{(G^{\prime})}[\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(x+z_{1}/N+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(y+z_{2}/N+\mathrm{i}\eta)]]]

(5.78)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{2}F(z_{1},z_{2})+N^{\varepsilon+\varepsilon_{1}}\mathcal{O}(\eta^{-1}T^{-1}).

The above will allow us to begin estimating the covariance. We have the following for the Gaussian ensembles.

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
G
be a matrix from the Gaussian ensemble. Then for any
1\geq\eta\geq N^{-2}
and
\varepsilon>0
and
x,y\in(-2+\kappa,2-\kappa)
we have,

(5.79)

N^{2}\left|\mathrm{Cov}_{G}(\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}\eta)],\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(y+\mathrm{i}\eta)])\right|\leq CN^{\varepsilon}\frac{1}{\eta(|x-y|+\eta)}

Moreover,

(5.80)

N\left|\mathbb{E}^{G}[\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(x+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\right|\leq C

Proof.
The first estimate follows from Theorem
3.6
. The second follows from writing the Poisson kernel as

(5.81)

P_{\eta}(x)=\chi_{1}(x)P_{\eta}(x)+(1-\chi_{1}(x))P_{\eta}(x)=f_{1}(x)+f_{2}(x).

where
\chi_{1}
is a function that is
1
on
(-2+\kappa/2,2-\kappa/2)
and
0
outside of
(-2-\kappa/4,2-\kappa/4)
. From Theorem
2.14
we have

(5.82)

\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}f_{1}(G)]-N\int f_{1}(x)\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\right|\leq C.

From Proposition
2.15
we have,

(5.83)

\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}f_{2}(G)]-N\int f_{2}(x)\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\right|\leq C.

The claim follows. ∎

We can now conclude the main technical estimate of this section.

Theorem 5.13
.

For any
\varepsilon>0
the following holds. Let
\eta=2^{-k}
and
k
satisfy,

(5.84)

\mathfrak{b}\log_{2}(N)\leq k\leq\log_{2}(N\mathfrak{b}^{-1}).

We have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}^{(\tilde{Z})}[N^{2}\mathbb{E}^{(H)}\big[\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(x+Z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(y+Z_{2}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\big]\right|

(5.85)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CN^{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{1}{\eta(|x-y|+\eta)}+\frac{1}{\eta T}\right).

Proof.
Thanks to Proposition
5.9
, it suffices to estimate the function
F(z_{1},z_{2})
. For
|z_{1}|+|z_{2}|\leq N^{\varepsilon/1000}
we have, by Proposition
5.11
,

\displaystyle\left|N^{2}F(z_{1},z_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\leq N^{2}\left|\mathrm{Cov}_{G}(\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(x+N^{-1}z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\eta)],\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(y+N^{-1}z_{2}+\mathrm{i}\eta)])\right|

\displaystyle+N^{2}\bigg|\mathbb{E}^{G}\big[\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(x+N^{-1}z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\big]

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{E}^{G}\big[\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(y+N^{-1}z_{2}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\big]\bigg|

(5.86)

\displaystyle+N^{\varepsilon}\frac{1}{\eta T}.

The claim now follows from Proposition
5.12
, as well as the estimates in Proposition
5.7
. ∎

Proof of Theorem
3.3
. By (
5
) and Proposition
5.2
we have,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}\varphi_{k}(H))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{I_{\kappa/2}^{2}}\left|f_{k}(x)f_{k}(y)N^{2}\mathbb{E}^{(\tilde{Z})}\big[\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(x+Z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(y+Z_{2}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\big]\right|\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(5.87)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle CN^{-100}\|\varphi\|_{2}^{2}

where

(5.88)

I_{a}=(-2+a,2-a)

and the support of
\varphi
is in
(-2+\kappa,2-\kappa)
. For
x,y\in I_{\kappa/2}
we see from Theorem
5.13
that

\displaystyle\int_{I_{\kappa/2}^{2}}\left|f_{k}(x)f_{k}(y)N^{2}\mathbb{E}^{(\tilde{Z})}[\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(x+Z_{1}+\mathrm{i}\eta)](\mathrm{Im}[(m_{N}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})(y+Z_{2}+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\right|\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(5.89)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\int_{I_{\kappa/2}^{2}}|f(x)f(y)|\left(\frac{1}{\eta(|x-y|+\eta)}+\frac{1}{\eta T}\right)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

Clearly,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta T}\int_{I_{\kappa/2}^{2}}|f_{k}(x)f_{k}(y)|\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta T}\int_{I_{\kappa/2}^{2}}|f_{k}(x)|^{2}+|f_{k}(y)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(5.90)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\eta T}\|f_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{\eta T}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}

where in the final inequality we used Proposition
5.1
. Since,

(5.91)

\int_{|x|<5}\frac{1}{\eta(|x-y|+\eta)}\mathrm{d}x\leq C\frac{|\log(\eta)|}{\eta}

for any
y
, we see that

\displaystyle\int_{I_{\kappa/2}^{2}}\frac{|f_{k}(x)f_{k}(y)|}{(x-y)^{2}+\eta^{2}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\leq

\displaystyle\int_{I_{\kappa/2}^{2}}\frac{|f_{k}(x)|^{2}}{(x-y)^{2}+\eta^{2}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(5.92)

\displaystyle\leq CN^{\varepsilon}\eta^{-1}\|f_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\leq CN^{\varepsilon}\eta^{-1}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}.

The result follows. ∎

6
Large frequencies

In this section we prove Theorems
3.1
and the variance and expectation estimates of Theorem
3.2
via resolvent expansions. Fix
\tau>0
and let us define,

(6.1)

\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{\tau,1}\cup\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\tau,1},

where
\mathcal{D}_{\tau,1}
is defined in (
2.9
). Recall the definition of the control parameter
\Psi(z)
in (
2.14
).

In the rest of this section we will use the notation,

(6.2)

z=x\pm\mathrm{i}y,\qquad w=u\pm\mathrm{i}v

where
y,v>0
.

The main tool is the following self-consistent equation for expectations of products of Stieltjes transforms.

Proposition 6.1
.

For
H
a real symmetric Wigner matrix and
z,w\in\mathcal{D}
we have for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle(z+2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{N^{2}}\partial_{w}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}+\frac{4s_{3}(m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)+m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w))}{N^{5/2}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{2s_{4}m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}}{N^{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3}(y^{-2}v^{-1}+(y+v)^{-1}v^{-2}))

(6.3)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3/2}v^{-1}(\Psi(z)^{2}\Psi(w)+\Psi(z)\Psi(w)^{2})+N^{-2}\Psi(z)\Psi(w)v^{-1})

The proof of this proposition is via cumulant expansions, and is given over the course of Section
6.1
.

6.1
Self-consistent equation for covariance

By the cumulant expansion we have for any
\varepsilon>0
that,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j}z\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j,a}\mathbb{E}[H_{ia}G_{ai}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j,a}\sum_{k=2}^{7}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{k-1}s_{k}}{(k-1)!N^{k/2}}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{k-1}(G_{ai}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)]))]

(6.4)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon-3}).

Here, the notation
\partial_{ia}
means the derivative with respect to the matrix entry
(i,a)
. Since we have symmetric matrices, note that this involves the derivative both with respect to the entry
(i,a)
and
(a,i)
.

For a treatment of the error term, see the discussion in
[
45
]
(the expansion with error term appears there as Lemma 3.2); there is no substantial difference from the treatment appearing there and here. We will refer to the
k
th term in the sum (
6.1
) as the
k
th-order term. Each of these terms is expanded and dealt with in each of the next few subsubsections. We will also require expressions for derivatives of resolvent entries with respect to matrix entries. These are collected in Appendix
A
.

6.1.1
Second order term

Lemma 6.2
.

We have for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{ija}(1+\delta_{ia})\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}(G_{ai}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)]))]

(6.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)\mathbb{E}[(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

(6.6)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{2}}\partial_{w}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}+\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon-3}(y^{-2}v^{-1}+(y+v)^{-1}v^{-2})

Proof.
We have, using (
A.2
) and (
A.3
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{ija}(1+\delta_{ia})\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}(G_{ai}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)]))]

(6.7)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{ija}\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)])]

(6.8)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{ija}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)^{2}(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)])]

(6.9)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{3}}\sum_{ija}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)G_{ja}(w)G_{ji}(w)]

The first term (
6.7
) equals,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{ija}\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)^{2}(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)]\mathbb{E}[(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]+\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon-3}y^{-2}v^{-1})

(6.10)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)\mathbb{E}[(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]+\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon-3}y^{-2}v^{-1}).

In the second to last line we wrote
m_{N}(z)=(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])+\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)]
, expanded the square and used the local law (
2.11
). In the last line we used again the local law (
2.11
).

The term (
6.8
) equals,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{ija}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)^{2}(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)])]=-\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{z}m_{N}(z)(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

(6.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{z}(m_{N}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]=\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon}N^{-3}y^{-2}v^{-1})

where we used the local law (
2.11
) and the Cauchy integral formula to obtain the last estimate. For the term (
6.9
) we have,

(6.12)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{3}}\sum_{ija}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)G_{ja}(w)G_{ji}(w)]=-2\partial_{w}\frac{1}{N^{3}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}G(z)G(w)].

We have the identity,

(6.13)

\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}G(z)G(w)=\frac{m_{N}(z)-m_{N}(w)}{z-w}.

If
z
and
w
are in opposite half-planes, then since
|z-w|\geq y+v
we have by the local law (
2.11
) with overwhelming probability,

\displaystyle\partial_{w}\frac{m_{N}(z)-m_{N}(w)}{z-w}=\frac{m_{N}(z)-m_{N}(w)}{(z-w)^{2}}-\frac{\partial_{w}m_{N}(w)}{(z-w)}

(6.14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{(z-w)^{2}}-\frac{\partial_{w}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}+\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon-1}(y^{-2}v^{-1}+(y+v)^{-1}v^{-2})

and the main term in the last line equals
\partial_{w}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}
. If
z
and
w
are in the same half-plane we consider first the case
10|z-w|\geq\max\{y,v\}
. In this case we use the same method as above to derive the same estimate. In the case that
10|z-w|\leq\max\{y,v\}
we see that
v\leq 2y\leq 4v
. We have the identity,

(6.15)

\frac{m_{N}(z)-m_{N}(w)}{z-w}=-\int_{0}^{1}m_{N}^{\prime}(z+s(w-z))\mathrm{d}s.

For the integrand we have,
m_{N}^{\prime}(z+s(w-z))=m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z+s(w-z))+\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon-1}y^{-2})
by the local law with overwhelming probability. Summarizing for (
6.9
) we have derived that with overwhelming probability,

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{N^{3}}\sum_{ija}G_{ia}(z)G_{ja}(w)G_{ji}(w)=

\displaystyle-2\partial_{w}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}

(6.16)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon-3}(y^{-2}v^{-1}+(y+v)^{-1}v^{-2})).

This yields the claim. ∎

6.1.2
Third order term

In the proof below, where possible, we will simplify errors that arise using

(6.17)

\frac{1}{N}\leq\Psi(E\pm\mathrm{i}\eta)\leq C\frac{1}{(N\eta)^{1/2}}

for
E\pm\mathrm{i}\eta\in\mathcal{D}
, to absorb them into error terms that have arisen already in the proof.

Lemma 6.3
.

We have,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3+1/2}}\sum_{ija}(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{2}(G_{ai}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)]))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)+m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)}{N^{5/2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3}y^{-2}v^{-1}+N^{-3}v^{-2}(y+v)^{-1})

(6.18)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3/2}v^{-1}(\Psi(z)^{2}\Psi(w)+\Psi(z)\Psi(w)^{2})+N^{-2}\Psi(z)\Psi(w)v^{-1}).

Proof.
We have, using (
A.4
), (
A.5
) and (
A.6
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3+1/2}}\sum_{ija}(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{2}(G_{ai}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)]))]

(6.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{2+1/2}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)^{3}(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

(6.20)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{6}{N^{2+1/2}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

(6.21)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{4}{N^{3+1/2}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)]

(6.22)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{4}{N^{3+1/2}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)^{2}\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)]

(6.23)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{4}{N^{3+1/2}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)G_{ai}(w)\partial_{w}G_{ai}(w)]

(6.24)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{4}{N^{3+1/2}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)G_{ii}(w)\partial_{w}G_{aa}(w)].

The first term (
6.19
) is,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{2+1/2}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)^{3}(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{2+1/2}}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[(G_{ii}(z)^{3}-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{3})(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]+\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon}N^{-3/2}\Psi(z)^{3}v^{-1})

(6.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-7/2}y^{-1}v^{-1}+N^{-3/2}\Psi(z)^{3}v^{-1})

In the second line we used (
2.12
) to estimate the off-diagonal Green’s function elements and also applied the local law (
2.11
). In the last line we expanded out
(G_{ii}-m_{\mathrm{sc}}+m_{\mathrm{sc}})^{3}
and used the local law (
2.11
) to estimate the term linear in
G_{ii}
and then the entry-wise law (
2.12
) to estimate the higher powers.

For the term (
6.20
) we first introduce the notation,

(6.26)

\mathbf{e}=N^{-1/2}(1,\dots,1)^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{N},

i.e., the constant unit vector in
\mathbb{R}^{N}
. Then, writing
G_{ii}=(G_{ii}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})+m_{\mathrm{sc}}
and expanding (and doing the same for
G_{aa}
) we find for (
6.20
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2+1/2}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{e}^{T}G(z)\mathbf{e}(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[(G_{ii}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))(G(z)\mathbf{e})_{i}(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

(6.27)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon}N^{-3/2}\Psi(z)^{3}v^{-1}).

By the isotropic local law (
2.13
) we see

(6.28)

\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{e}^{T}G(z)\mathbf{e}(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-5/2}\Psi(z)v^{-1}).

By the isotropic local law (
2.13
) we see with overwhelming probability that,

(6.29)

(G(z)\mathbf{e})_{i}=N^{-1/2}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(\Psi(z))

and so,

\displaystyle\frac{2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}{N^{2}}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[(G_{ii}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))(G(z)\mathbf{e})_{i}(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}}{N^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}[(m_{N}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-2}\Psi(z)^{2}v^{-1})

(6.30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(N^{-7/2}y^{-1}v^{-1}+N^{-2}\Psi(z)^{2}v^{-1}).

This completes estimating the term (
6.20
). We write (
6.21
) as,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3+1/2}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}}{N^{5/2}}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{w}\mathbf{e}^{T}G(w)\mathbf{e}]+\frac{2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}{N^{3}}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[(G_{ii}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))\partial_{w}(G(w)\mathbf{e})_{i}]

(6.31)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{7/2}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(G_{ii}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))(G_{aa}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)].

By the isotropic local law (
2.13
) and the Cauchy integral formula, we have

(6.32)

\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}}{N^{5/2}}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{w}\mathbf{e}^{T}G(w)\mathbf{e}]=\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)}{N^{5/2}}+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-5/2}\Psi(w)v^{-1}).

By (
6.29
) with
z
replaced by
w
and the entry-wise local law (
2.12
) we have,

(6.33)

\frac{2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}{N^{3}}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[(G_{ii}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))\partial_{w}(G(w)\mathbf{e})_{i}]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3}y^{-1}v^{-1/2}+N^{-2}\Psi(z)\Psi(w)v^{-1}),

where we used a crude bound
|m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)|\leq Cv^{-1/2}
. Finally, after separating into diagonal and off-diagonal terms we find,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{7/2}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(G_{ii}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))(G_{aa}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)]

(6.34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3/2}\Psi(z)^{2}\Psi(w)v^{-1}+N^{-5/2}\Psi(z)^{2}(\Psi(w)v^{-1}+v^{-1/2})),

again using a crude bound
|m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)|\leq Cv^{-1/2}
.
This completes (
6.21
). We now turn to (
6.22
). Clearly,

(6.35)

\displaystyle\frac{4}{N^{3+1/2}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)^{2}\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)]=\frac{4}{N^{7/2}}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}(z)^{2}\partial_{w}G_{ii}(w)]+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3/2}\Psi(z)^{2}\Psi(w)v^{-1}),

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{7/2}}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}(z)^{2}\partial_{w}G_{ii}(w)]

(6.36)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)}{N^{5/2}}+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-7/2}(y^{-1}v^{-1/2}+v^{-2})+N^{-5/2}(\Psi(z)\Psi(w)v^{-1}),

by expanding
G_{ii}(z)=(G_{ii}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))+m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)
(and similarly for
G_{ii}(w)
), using the local law (
2.11
) to estimate the terms linear in
G_{ii}
, and the entrywise local law (
2.12
) for the terms quadratic and cubic in
G_{ii}
. We also used the crude bound
|m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)|\leq v^{-1/2}
.

This completes the treatment of (
6.22
). For (
6.23
), the diagonal terms are handled similarly to (
6.1.2
) and we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{7/2}}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}(z)G_{ii}(w)\partial_{w}G_{ii}(w)]

(6.37)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)}{N^{5/2}}+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-7/2}(y^{-1}v^{-1/2}+v^{-2})+N^{-5/2}\Psi(z)\Psi(w)v^{-1}).

The off-diagonal terms are
N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3/2}\Psi(z)\Psi(w)^{2}v^{-1})
. We write (
6.24
) as,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{7/2}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)G_{ii}(w)\partial_{w}G_{aa}(w)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{N^{5/2}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{e}^{T}G(z)\mathbf{e}]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)}{N^{3}}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[(G(z)\mathbf{e})_{i}(G_{ii}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))]+\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}{N^{3}}\mathbb{E}[(G(z)\mathbf{e})_{i}\partial_{w}(G_{ii}(w)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w))]

(6.38)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3/2}v^{-1}\Psi(w)^{2}\Psi(z)).

By the isotropic local law (
2.13
) the first term is,

(6.39)

\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{N^{5/2}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{e}^{T}G(z)\mathbf{e}]=\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{N^{5/2}}+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-5/2}\Psi(z)v^{-1/2}).

Using (
6.29
) as well as the local law (
2.11
) we obtain easily that,

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)}{N^{3}}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[(G(z)\mathbf{e})_{i}(G_{ii}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))]+\sum_{i}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}{N^{3}}\mathbb{E}[(G(z)\mathbf{e})_{i}\partial_{w}(G_{ii}(w)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w))]

(6.40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-7/2}y^{-1}v^{-1/2}+N^{-2}\Psi(z)^{2}v^{-1/2}+N^{-7/2}v^{-2}+N^{-2}\Psi(z)\Psi(w)v^{-1})

Adding up all the errors and simplifying yields the claim. ∎

6.1.3
Fourth order term

Lemma 6.4
.

We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{ija}(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{3}(G_{ai}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)]))]

(6.41)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{12m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}}{N^{2}}+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3}(v^{-1}y^{-1}+v^{-2})+N^{-2}\Psi(z)\Psi(w)v^{-1})

Proof.
We break up the left side of (
6.4
) into the four terms based on how the three derivatives are distributed amongst the
G_{ia}(z)
and
G_{jj}(w)
.
We first calculate, using (
A.7
),

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{ia}(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{ia}^{3}G_{ai}(z))(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(6G_{ia}^{4}(z)+36G_{ia}(z)^{2}G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z))(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

(6.42)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{6}{N^{3}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}^{2}(z)G_{aa}(z)^{2}(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])].

Clearly for the second line,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(6G_{ia}^{4}(z)+36G_{ia}(z)^{2}G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z))(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

(6.43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-2}v^{-1}\Psi(z)^{2}+N^{-3}v^{-1}).

For the last term of (
6.42
) we have,

(6.44)

\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}^{2}(z)G_{aa}(z)^{2}(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3}y^{-1}v^{-1})

where we again expanded the resolvent entries around
m_{\mathrm{sc}}
and used the local law (
2.11
) to estimate the terms linear in
G_{ii}
or
G_{aa}
.

The next term is, using (
A.4
) and (
A.3
),

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{ija}(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{ia}^{2}G_{ia}(z))(\partial_{ia}G_{jj}(w))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w))G_{ia}(z)^{3}]

(6.45)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{12}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w))G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)G_{ia}(z)]

For the first term, we have

(6.46)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w))G_{ia}(z)^{3}]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-2}\Psi(z)^{3}\Psi(w)v^{-1}+N^{-3}v^{-1})

where we used a crude bound
|\partial_{w}G_{ii}(w)|\leq Cv^{-1}
for the diagonal entries, which holds with overwhelming probability. For the term on the last line of (
6.1.3
) we have by the same argument,

(6.47)

\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w))G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)G_{ia}(z)]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-2}\Psi(z)\Psi(w)v^{-1}+N^{-3}v^{-1}).

For the next term contributing to the LHS of (
6.4
) we have, using (
A.2
) and (
A.6
),

\displaystyle\frac{3}{N^{4}}\sum_{ija}(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{ia}G_{ia}(z))(\partial_{ia}^{2}G_{jj}(w))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{12}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ii}(w))G_{aa}(w)G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{12}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ii}(w))G_{aa}(w)G_{ia}(z)^{2}]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{12}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w))G_{ia}(w)G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)]

(6.48)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{12}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w))G_{ia}(w)G_{ia}(z)^{2}]

We begin with the first term on the right side of (
6.1.3
). By expanding each resolvent entry around
m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)
or
m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)
and using the local law (
2.11
) to estimate the terms that are linear in the
G_{ii}
and the entrywise law (
2.12
) to estimate the terms quadratic or higher in the
G_{ii}
we obtain,

\displaystyle-\frac{12}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ii}(w))G_{aa}(w)G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)]

(6.49)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{12m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}}{N^{2}}+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3}(y^{-1}v^{-1/2}+v^{-2})+N^{-2}(v^{-1}\Psi(w)\Psi(z))).

For the second term on the right side of (
6.1.3
) we have,

(6.50)

\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ii}(w))G_{aa}(w)G_{ia}(z)^{2}]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3}y^{-1}v^{-1}).

The third term is,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w))G_{ia}(w)G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3}}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{w}^{2}m_{N}(w)]+\frac{2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}{N^{4}}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}^{2}G_{ii}(w))(G_{ii}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))]

(6.51)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-2}\Psi(z)^{2}\Psi(w)^{2}v^{-1}+N^{-3}v^{-1}).

Clearly,

(6.52)

\frac{1}{N^{3}}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{w}^{2}m_{N}(w)]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3}v^{-2}),

and

(6.53)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}^{2}G_{ii}(w))(G_{ii}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3}v^{-2}).

The last term on the right side of (
6.1.3
) is,

(6.54)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w))G_{ia}(w)G_{ia}(z)^{2}]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-2}\Psi(z)^{2}\Psi(w)^{2}v^{-1}+N^{-3}v^{-1})

The final contribution to the left side of (
6.4
) is, using (
A.8
),

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{ija}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)(\partial_{ia}^{3}G_{jj}(w))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{24}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)G_{ai}(w)G_{ii}(w)\partial_{w}G_{aa}(w)]

(6.55)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{12}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w))G_{ia}(z)(G_{aa}(w)G_{ii}(w)+G_{ia}(w)^{2})].

By splitting into off-diagonal and diagonal terms, we see that the first term is

(6.56)

\frac{24}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)G_{ai}(w)G_{ii}(w)\partial_{w}G_{aa}(w)]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-2}\Psi(z)\Psi(w)v^{-1}+N^{-3}v^{-1})

and the second term is

(6.57)

\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w))G_{ia}(z)(G_{aa}(w)G_{ii}(w)+G_{ia}(w)^{2})]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-2}\Psi(z)\Psi(w)v^{-1}+N^{-3}v^{-1}).

This completes the proof. ∎

6.1.4
Fifth order

Lemma 6.5
.

We have,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{9/2}}\sum_{ija}(1+\delta_{ia})^{4}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{4}(G_{ai}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)])]

(6.58)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-5/2}\Psi(z)v^{-1}+N^{-5/2}\Psi(w)v^{-1/2}).

Proof.
We write the term as,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{9/2}}\sum_{ija}(1+\delta_{ia})^{4}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{4}(G_{ai}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{7/2}}\sum_{ia}(1+\delta_{ia})^{4}\mathbb{E}[(\partial^{4}_{ia}G_{ai}(z)(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

(6.59)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{9/2}}\sum_{n=0}^{3}c_{n}\sum_{ia}(1+\delta_{ia})^{4}\mathbb{E}[(\partial^{3-n}_{ia}G_{ai}(z))(\partial_{w}\partial_{ia}^{n}G_{ia}(w))]

for some combinatorial constants
c_{n}
. Consider the first term on the right side of (
6.1.4
). The expression
(1+\delta_{ia})^{4}\partial_{ia}^{4}G_{ia}
is a linear combination of products of five resolvent entries. Among five resolvent entries there are 10 indices, 5 of which must be
i
and 5 of which must be
a
. Therefore, each term contains at least one off-diagonal entry
G_{ia}
. Therefore, with overwhelming probability we have,

(6.60)

|\partial_{ia}^{4}G_{ia}|\leq C\delta_{ia}+N^{\varepsilon}\Psi(z).

It follows that the second line of (
6.1.4
) is
\mathcal{O}(N^{-5/2}\Psi(z)v^{-1})
. By the same parity argument, we find that for any derivative of even order
n=2m
there exists
C
such that

(6.61)

|\partial_{ia}^{2m}G_{ia}|\leq C\delta_{ia}+N^{\varepsilon}\Psi(z),

with overwhelming probability. Therefore, for any
n=0,1,2,3
we have

(6.62)

|\partial^{3-n}_{ia}G_{ai}(z))(\partial_{w}\partial_{ia}^{n}G_{ia}(w)|\leq N^{\varepsilon}v^{-1}(\delta_{ia}+\Psi(z)+\Psi(w)),

with overwhelming probability. It follows that the last line of (
6.1.4
) is
N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-5/2}\Psi(z)v^{-1}+N^{-5/2}\Psi(w)v^{-1/2})
. This yields the claim. ∎

6.1.5
Sixth and seventh order

Lemma 6.6
.

We have,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{5}}\sum_{ija}(1+\delta_{ia})^{5}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{5}(G_{ai}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)])]

(6.63)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3}v^{-1}).

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{11/2}}\sum_{ija}(1+\delta_{ia})^{6}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{6}(G_{ai}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)])]

(6.64)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3}v^{-1}).

Proof.
We consider,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{5}}\sum_{ija}(1+\delta_{ia})^{5}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{5}(G_{ai}(z)(G_{jj}(w)-\mathbb{E}[G_{jj}(w)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{ia}(1+\delta_{ia})^{5}\mathbb{E}[(\partial^{5}_{ia}G_{ai}(z)(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

(6.65)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{5}}\sum_{n=0}^{4}c_{n}\sum_{ia}(1+\delta_{ia})^{5}\mathbb{E}[(\partial^{4-n}_{ia}G_{ai}(z))(\partial_{w}\partial_{ia}^{n}G_{ia}(w))]

for some combinatorial constants
c_{n}
.
We easily conclude the proof from, the fact that for any
n
we have

(6.66)

|\partial_{ia}^{k}G_{ia}|\leq C,\qquad|\partial_{ia}^{k}\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)|\leq Cv^{-1},

with overwhelming probability. The result for the seventh order term is proven in the same way. ∎

6.1.6
Proof of Proposition
6.1

This follows from the expansion (
6.1
); in each of the previous few subsections, the lemmas provide the required estimates for each of the terms in the expansion. ∎

6.2
Covariance estimates

Recall the notation,

(6.67)

\kappa(E)=||E|-2|.

For
z=x+\mathrm{i}y
we define
\kappa(z)=\kappa(x)
.
Since

(6.68)

|z+2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)|\asymp\sqrt{\kappa+y},

we see from Proposition
6.1
and Lemma
B.2
that,

\displaystyle N^{2}\mathbb{E}[(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)}{(1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w))^{2}}-4s_{3}N^{-1/2}m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)(m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)+m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w))

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2s_{4}m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\kappa(x)+y}}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3}(y^{-2}v^{-1}+(y+v)^{-1}v^{-2}))

(6.69)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\kappa(x)+y}}\mathcal{O}(N^{-3/2}v^{-1}(\Psi(z)^{2}\Psi(w)+\Psi(z)\Psi(w)^{2})+N^{-2}\Psi(z)\Psi(w)v^{-1})

From this, we conclude the following proof.

Proof of Theorem
3.1
. We apply (
6.2
) assuming that
\sqrt{\kappa(x)+y}\geq\sqrt{\kappa(u)+v}
. Otherwise, we reverse the roles of
z
and
w
. Since
v,y\geq N^{-1/2}
we have,

(6.70)

\Psi(z)\leq C\frac{(\kappa(x)+y)^{1/4}}{(Ny)^{1/2}},

and a similar estimate for
\Psi(w)
.
This yields the claim. ∎

Let
\varphi
be a function supported in
[-5,5]
and recall the following quasi-analytic extension of
\varphi
,

(6.71)

\tilde{\varphi}(x+\mathrm{i}y)=(\varphi(x)+\mathrm{i}y\varphi^{\prime}(x))\chi(y),

where
\chi
is smooth even bump function that is on
[-1,1]
and
0
outside of
[-2,2]
. Recall the formula

(6.72)

\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{\varphi}(x+\mathrm{i}y)=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{i}y\chi(y)\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)+\mathrm{i}(\varphi(x)+\mathrm{i}\varphi^{\prime}(x)y)\chi^{\prime}(y),

as well as the Helffer-Sjöstrand (HS) formula
[
7
, Appendix C]

(6.73)

\mathrm{tr}\varphi(H)-\mathbb{E}[\varphi(H)]=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{\varphi}(x+\mathrm{i}y)N(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y.

Recall the definition of the norm
\|\varphi\|_{1,w}
in (
3.2
).
The following is a simple consequence of the HS formula.

Lemma 6.7
.

Assume that
\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}\leq N
and let
\varepsilon>0
. With overwhelming probability we have,

(6.74)

\left|\mathrm{tr}\varphi(H)-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}\varphi(H)]\right|\leq N^{\varepsilon}(1+\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1}+\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}N^{-1})

Proof.
We have by a direct application of the HS formula and the local law (
2.11
),

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{tr}\varphi(H)-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}\varphi(H)]\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}y\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y)N(m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)])\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|

(6.75)

\displaystyle+CN^{\varepsilon}(\|\varphi\|_{1}+\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1}).

For the term on the first line, by the symmetry of
\chi(y)
we have,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}y\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y)N(m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)])\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int_{y>0}y\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y)N\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)]]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

We have that
y\to y\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)]
is increasing and so using the local law (
2.11
) it follows that for
0<y<N^{\varepsilon-1}
that,

(6.76)

yN\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)]\leq N^{\varepsilon}

with overwhelming probability and so,

(6.77)

\left|\int_{y<N^{\varepsilon-1}}y\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y)N\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)]]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|\leq CN^{2\varepsilon-1}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}

Let
t=N^{\varepsilon}(1+\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1})/(\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|)
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{N^{\varepsilon-1}<y<t}y\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y)N\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)]]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|

(6.78)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{y<t}CN^{\varepsilon}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}\mathrm{d}y\leq CN^{\varepsilon}(1+\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1}).

For larger
y
, we have by integration by parts and the compact support of
\varphi
that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{y>t}y\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)\chi(y)N\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)]]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{y>t}y\varphi^{\prime}(x)\chi(y)N\mathrm{Im}[m^{\prime}_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)-\mathbb{E}[m^{\prime}_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}y)]]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|

(6.79)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\int_{3>|y|>t}\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1}y^{-1}\mathrm{d}y\leq CN^{2\varepsilon}\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1}

where we used the Cauchy integral formula. ∎

Following
[
44
]
, define

(6.80)

\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}:=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:|y|>N^{\mathfrak{a}-1}\}

where
0<\mathfrak{a}<1
.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
\varepsilon>0,\mathfrak{a}>0
and
H
be a Wigner matrix. With overwhelming probability,

(6.81)

\displaystyle\left|(\mathrm{tr}\varphi(H)-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}\varphi(H)])-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{\varphi}(z)N(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|\leq CN^{\varepsilon}N^{\mathfrak{a}-1}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}.

Proof.
This is a straightforward conseqeunce of the local law. The argument of the previous lemma shows that the contribution from
|y|<N^{\varepsilon-1}
is
\mathcal{O}(N^{2\varepsilon-1}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1})
. The contribution from
N^{\varepsilon-1}<|y|<N^{\mathfrak{a}-1}
is
\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon+\mathfrak{a}-1}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1})
due to the local law (
2.11
) and the explicit form of the quasi-analytic extension
\varphi
. ∎

Theorem 6.9
.

Assume
\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}\leq N
.
We have for any
\varepsilon>0
,

(6.82)

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}\varphi(H))-V(\varphi)\right|\leq N^{\varepsilon-2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}^{2}+N^{\varepsilon-1}(1+\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1,w})\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}

where,

\displaystyle V(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{-2}^{2}\frac{(\varphi(x)-\varphi(u))^{2}}{(x-u)^{2}}\frac{4-xu}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}\sqrt{4-u^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle+\frac{s_{4}}{2\pi^{2}}\left(\int_{-2}^{2}\varphi(x)\frac{2-x^{2}}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\right)^{2}

(6.83)

\displaystyle-\frac{2s_{3}}{\pi^{2}N^{1/2}}\left(\int_{-2}^{2}\varphi(x)\frac{2-x^{2}}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\right)\left(\int_{-2}^{2}\varphi(x)\frac{x}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\right)

Proof.
By the previous two lemmas we have,

(6.84)
