\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}\varphi(H))=\frac{1}{\pi^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{\varphi}N(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y)\right)^{2}\right]+\mathcal{O}(N^{2\mathfrak{a}+\varepsilon-1}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}(1+\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1}))

The first term on the right side is,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{\varphi}N(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y)\right)^{2}\right]

(6.85)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{\varphi}(z)\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{\varphi}(w)N^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])\big]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

where we use the notation
z=x+\mathrm{i}y
and
w=u+\mathrm{i}v
.
Let
F(z,w)
be the function defined by the second and third lines of (
6.2
) so that,

\displaystyle|H_{N}(z,w)|:=

\displaystyle\left|N^{2}\mathbb{E}[(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w)])]-F(z,w)\right|

(6.86)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\kappa(x)+|y|}}\left(\frac{1}{Ny^{2}|v|}+\frac{1}{Nv^{2}|y|}\right).

We simplified the errors in (
6.2
) using the upper bound of (
6.17
).
We now use this estimate to replace the terms
m_{N}(z),m_{N}(w)
with
F(z,w)
in the above integral expression for the variance.
Clearly,

(6.87)

\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}|\chi^{\prime}(y)\chi^{\prime}(v)(\varphi(x)+\mathrm{i}y\varphi^{\prime}(x))(\varphi(u)+\mathrm{i}v\varphi^{\prime}(u))H_{N}(z,w)|\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\leq CN^{\varepsilon-1}(1+\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1,w})^{2}

and

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}|\chi(y)\chi^{\prime}(v)y\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)(\varphi(u)+\mathrm{i}v\varphi^{\prime}(u))H_{N}(z,w)|\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

(6.88)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CN^{\varepsilon-1}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)\|_{1,w}(1+\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1,w})\int_{N^{-1}<|y|<1}|y|^{-1}\mathrm{d}y\leq CN^{2\varepsilon-1}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}(1+\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1,w}).

Define
t=N^{\varepsilon}(1+\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1,w})\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}^{-1}\wedge 1
. We have,

\displaystyle\int_{|y|,|v|<t}|\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)\varphi^{\prime\prime}(v)yvH_{N}(z,w)|\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\leq

\displaystyle\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}^{2}N^{\varepsilon-1}\int_{|y|,|v|<t}|y|^{-1}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y

(6.89)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CN^{3\varepsilon-1}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}(1+\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1,w}).

By integration by parts in the
x
variable, and the fact that
z\to H_{N}(z,w)
is holomorphic away from the real axis and the Cauchy integral formula,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|y|>t,|v|<t}\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)y\chi^{\prime}(y)\varphi^{\prime\prime}(u)v\chi^{\prime}(v)H_{N}(z,w)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|y|>t,|v|<t}\varphi^{\prime}(x)y\chi^{\prime}(y)\varphi^{\prime\prime}(u)v\chi^{\prime}(v)\partial_{z}H_{N}(z,w)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\right|

(6.90)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1,w}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}\int_{|y|>t,|v|<t}(y^{-2}+|yv|^{-1})\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}v\leq CN^{2\varepsilon}\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1,w}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}.

Finally,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|y|>t,|v|>t}\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)y\chi^{\prime}(y)\varphi^{\prime\prime}(u)v\chi^{\prime}(v)H_{N}(z,w)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|y|>t,|v|>t}\varphi^{\prime}(x)y\chi^{\prime}(y)\varphi^{\prime}(u)v\chi^{\prime}(v)\partial_{z}\partial_{w}H_{N}(z,w)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\right|

(6.91)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon-1}\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1,w}^{2}\int_{|y|,|v|>t}y^{-2}|v|^{-1}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}y\leq N^{2\varepsilon-1}\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1,w}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}

The above estimates show that,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{\varphi}(z)\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{\varphi}(w)N^{2}\mathbb{E}[(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])(m_{N}(w)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(w))]

(6.92)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{\varphi}(z)\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{\varphi}(w)F(z,w)+N^{3\varepsilon-1}\mathcal{O}(\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}(1+\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1,w})).

Using the estimate,

(6.93)

|F(z,w)|\leq C\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa(x)+y}\sqrt{\kappa(u)+v}}\frac{1}{y^{2}+v^{2}}

it is straightforward to conclude that,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{\varphi}(z)\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{\varphi}(w)F(z,w)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{C}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{\varphi}(z)\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{\varphi}(w)F(z,w)

(6.94)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{2\mathfrak{a}-2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}^{2}+N^{\mathfrak{a}-1}\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{1,w}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w})

The integral on the right side may then be calculated by Green’s theorem which in complex notation states that

(6.95)

\int_{\Omega}\partial_{\bar{z}}F(z)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\int_{\partial\Omega}F(z)\mathrm{d}z.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{\varphi}(z)\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{\varphi}(w)F(z,w)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

(6.96)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi(x)\varphi(y)(F(x+\mathrm{i}0,y+\mathrm{i}0)+F(x-\mathrm{i}0,y-\mathrm{i}0)-2F(x+\mathrm{i}0,y-\mathrm{i}0))\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y.

The contributions from the terms that do not involve
s_{3}
were calculated in the proof of
[
44
, Lemma 4.2]
. The term that involves
s_{3}
may be calculated as

\displaystyle-\frac{8s_{3}}{N^{1/2}\pi^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{\varphi}(z)\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{\varphi}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{8s_{3}}{N^{1/2}\pi^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi(x)\mathrm{Im}[m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(x+\mathrm{i}0)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(x+\mathrm{i}0)]\mathrm{d}x\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi(y)\mathrm{Im}[m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(y+\mathrm{i}0)]\mathrm{d}y\right)

(6.97)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{8s_{3}}{N^{1/2}\pi^{2}}\left(\int_{-2}^{2}\varphi(x)\frac{x^{2}-2}{2\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\right)\left(\int_{-2}^{2}\varphi(y)\frac{-y}{2\sqrt{4-y^{2}}}\mathrm{d}y\right)

where the last line follows from the explicit formula of
m_{\mathrm{sc}}
.
We conclude the proof. ∎

6.3
Expectation

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
H
be a Wigner matrix. Let
z=x+\mathrm{i}y
. Then,

\displaystyle(z+2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))(\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)]-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{N}m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)

(6.98)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{4s_{3}}{N^{3/2}}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{3}-\frac{s_{4}}{N}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{4}

(6.99)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}((Ny)^{-2}).

Proof.
By the cumulant expansion,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}z\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}(z)]+1=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[H_{ia}G_{ia}(z)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)^{2}]-\partial_{z}\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{n=3}^{5}\frac{1}{N^{1+n/2}}\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{n-1}s_{n}}{(n-1)!}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{n}G_{ia}]

(6.100)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-2}).

By the local law (
2.11
) we have,

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)^{2}]-\frac{1}{N}\partial_{z}\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)]

\displaystyle=-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}-2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)]-\frac{1}{N}\partial_{z}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)

(6.101)

\displaystyle+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}((Ny)^{-2}).

By (
A.4
) and similar arguments as in the proof of Lemma
6.3
, we have that

(6.102)

\frac{1}{N^{5/2}}\sum_{ia}(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{2}G_{ia}]=\frac{8}{N^{3/2}}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{3}+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}((Ny)^{-2}).

From (
A.7
) and the entrywise local law (
2.12
) we see,

(6.103)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{ia}(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{3}G_{ia}]=-\frac{6}{N^{3}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}^{2}G_{aa}^{2}]+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}((Ny)^{-2}).

Expanding
G_{ii}=(G_{ii}-m_{\mathrm{sc}})+m_{\mathrm{sc}}
(and similarly for
G_{aa}
) and estimating the terms linear in
G_{ii}
using the local law (
2.11
) and the higher order terms using the entry-wise local law (
2.12
) we see that,

(6.104)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}^{2}G_{aa}^{2}]=\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{4}}{N}+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}((Ny)^{-2}).

Finally, the term with
n=5
in (
6.3
) is estimate using the parity estimate (
6.60
). ∎

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
f
be a function, and
H
a Wigner matrix. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}f(H)]

\displaystyle=N\int f(x)\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\mathrm{d}x-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int f(x)\frac{1}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\frac{f(2)+f(-2)}{4}+\frac{s_{4}}{2\pi}\int_{-2}^{2}f(x)\frac{x^{4}-4x^{2}+2}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x

(6.105)

\displaystyle+\frac{2s_{3}}{N^{1/2}}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{-2}^{2}f(x)\frac{3x-x^{3}}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w})

Proof.
The following is proven in a straightforward manner from the HS formula and Lemma
6.10
, similar to the argument proving Theorem
6.9
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}f(H)]-N\int f(x)\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{f}(z)\frac{-m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)-s_{4}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{4}+4s_{3}N^{-1/2}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{3}}{z+2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(6.106)

\displaystyle+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{\mathfrak{a}-1}\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w})

where
\tilde{f}
is as in (
6.71
) and
\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}
is as in (
6.80
). From the estimate,

(6.107)

\left|\frac{-m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)}{z+2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}\right|C\leq\frac{1}{\kappa(x)+y},

we see that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{f}(z)\frac{-m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)-s_{4}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{4}+4s_{3}N^{-1/2}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{3}}{z+2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(6.108)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{C}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{f}(z)\frac{-m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)-s_{4}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{4}+4s_{3}N^{-1/2}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{3}}{z+2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{\mathfrak{a}-1}\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}).

The integral without the
s_{3}N^{-1/2}
term as calculated in
[
44
, Lemma 4.3]
. Calculating the additional term is similar to what appears there, and the result follows.
∎

7
Mesoscopic estimates for Dyson Brownian Motion

In this section, we will consider Dyson Brownian Motion (DBM),

(7.1)

\mathrm{d}\lambda_{i}(t)=\sqrt{\frac{2}{N\beta}}\mathrm{d}B_{i}(t)+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{1}{\lambda_{i}(t)-\lambda_{j}(t)}\mathrm{d}t-\frac{\lambda_{i}(t)}{2}\mathrm{d}t

where the initial data are the eigenvalues of a Wigner matrix. Compared to (
1.33
) we introduced the parameter
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
above which equals
\beta=1
in the real symmetric case and
\beta=2
in the complex Hermitian case. The parameter does not play a substantial role but we introduce it for completeness.

We will consider the evolution of

(7.2)

s_{N}(z,t):=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\log(\lambda_{i}(t)-z)

for
z
in the upper half-plane. By the Itô formula,

(7.3)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}s_{N}=\frac{-m_{N}^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{2N}\left(1-\frac{2}{\beta}\right)m_{N}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{zm_{N}(z)}{2}+\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta}}\sum_{i}\frac{\mathrm{d}B_{i}}{\lambda_{i}-z}.

Define now,

(7.4)

\displaystyle s(z):=\int\log(x-z)\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\mathrm{d}x.

The characteristics as defined in (
2.2
) go backwards in time. We need the characteristics that go forward in time. For this, we fix a final time
T
and define

(7.5)

\tilde{z}_{t}:=z_{T-t}

for some final condition
\tilde{z}_{T}=z_{0}=z
which we suppress from the notation. Note that due to (
2.24
),
\tilde{z}_{s}
satisfies

(7.6)

\partial_{s}\tilde{z}_{s}=-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{s})-\frac{\tilde{z}_{s}}{2}.

Then, with
s(z)
as in (
7.4
),

(7.7)

\partial_{s}s(\tilde{z}_{s})=m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{s})^{2}+\tilde{z}_{s}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{s})}{2}=\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{s})^{2}-1}{2},

where we used
m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{2}+zm_{\mathrm{sc}}+1=0
. Plugging in the characteristic, we see that

(7.8)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}s_{N}(\tilde{z}_{s})

\displaystyle=-m_{N}^{2}/2+\frac{1}{2N}\left(1-\frac{2}{\beta}\right)m_{N}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}-\tilde{z}_{s}m_{N}/2+\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta}}\sum_{i}\frac{\mathrm{d}B_{i}}{\lambda_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}}

(7.9)

\displaystyle+m_{N}m_{\mathrm{sc}}+m_{N}\tilde{z}_{s}/2.

In Section
7.1
we estimate the difference of
\mathrm{d}s_{N}(\tilde{z}_{s})-\partial_{s}s(\tilde{z}_{s})
and use this to find an estimate of
s_{N}(\tilde{z}_{T},T)
in terms of
s_{N}(\tilde{z}_{0},0)
and an explicit Gaussian random variable. In Section
7.2
we calculate the variance of this Gaussian random variable as well as the covariance between two of these variables for different characteristics that are well-separated.

Before beginning, we record the following easy consequence of rigidity.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
\tau>0
. With overwhelming probability we have simultaneously for all
t\in[0,1]
and
z\in\mathcal{D}_{\tau,1}
that,

(7.10)

|m_{N}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)|\leq\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N\mathrm{Im}[z]}

as well as

(7.11)

|\lambda_{i}(t)-\gamma_{i}|\leq\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{2/3}\min\{i^{1/3},(N+1-i)^{1/3}\}}.

7.1
Estimates along characteristics

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
\tilde{z}_{s}
,
s\in[0,T]
be a characteristic, and assume that the final condition
z=\tilde{z}_{T}
satisfies
\mathrm{Re}[z]\in(-2+\kappa,2-\kappa)
and
\mathrm{Im}[z]\geq N^{\tau-1}
for some
\tau,\kappa>0
. Assume
T<(\log(N))^{-1}
. Let
\varepsilon>0
. With overwhelming probability,

\displaystyle s_{N}(\tilde{z}_{T},T)-s(\tilde{z}_{T})

\displaystyle=s_{N}(\tilde{z}_{0},0)-s(\tilde{z}_{0})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta}}\sum_{i}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{d}B_{i}(s)}{\gamma_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2N}\left(1-\frac{2}{\beta}\right)\int_{0}^{T}m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(\tilde{z}_{s})\mathrm{d}s

(7.12)

\displaystyle+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}\mathrm{Im}[z_{T}]}\right)

Proof.
Using (
7.6
) and (
7.8
) we have,

(7.13)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}(s_{N}(\tilde{z}_{s})-s(\tilde{z}_{s}))

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}(m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{s})-m_{N}(\tilde{z}_{s}))^{2}

(7.14)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2N}\left(1-\frac{2}{\beta}\right)(m_{N}^{\prime}(\tilde{z}_{s})-m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(\tilde{z}_{s}))

(7.15)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2N}\left(1-\frac{2}{\beta}\right)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(\tilde{z}_{s})

(7.16)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta}}\sum_{i}\mathrm{d}B_{i}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}}-\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}}\right)

(7.17)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta}}\sum_{i}\frac{\mathrm{d}B_{i}}{\gamma_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}}.

We now estimate the integrals in
s
of the various terms on the right. By the local law estimates of Proposition
7.1
we have, with overwhelming probability,

(7.18)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left|m_{N}(\tilde{z}_{s})-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{s})\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq N^{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{N^{2}(\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}])^{2}}\mathrm{d}s.

From the explicit form of the characteristics we see that,

(7.19)

c\leq-\partial_{s}\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]\leq c^{-1}

for some
c>0
depending on
\kappa>0
and our assumption that
T<(\log(N))^{-1}
. Therefore, for the term (
7.13
) we have the estimate,

(7.20)

\int_{0}^{T}\left|m_{N}(\tilde{z}_{s})-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{s})\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq N^{\varepsilon}\frac{1}{N^{2}\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}]}.

By the Cauchy integral formula we have for all
s\in[0,T]
,

(7.21)

\left|m_{N}^{\prime}(\tilde{z}_{s})-m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(\tilde{z}_{s})\right|\leq\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]^{2}}

and so we arrive at the same estimate for the time integral of (
7.14
) as we did for (
7.13
). For the term (
7.16
), we have by the rigidity estimates of Proposition
7.1
that,

(7.22)

\sum_{i}\left|\frac{1}{\lambda_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}}-\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N(\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}])^{3}}

with overwhelming probability for all
s\in[0,T]
. Therefore, by the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality:

(7.23)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta}}\sum_{i}\mathrm{d}B_{i}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}}-\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}}\right)\right|\leq\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{N^{2}\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}]}

with overwhelming probability. The claim follows. ∎

7.2
Covariance calculation

We now calculate the variance of the Gaussian random variables appearing in Proposition
7.2
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
\tilde{z}_{s}
be a characteristic whose final condition
\tilde{z}_{T}
satisfies
\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{z}_{T}]\in(-2+\kappa,2-\kappa)
and
\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}]\geq N^{\tau-1}
. Assume that
T<\log(N)^{-1}
. Then,

\displaystyle 2\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]^{2}}{|\gamma_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}|^{4}}\mathrm{d}s=

\displaystyle\log(\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{0}]/\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}])

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{T}{2}+\mathrm{Re}[\log(1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{T})^{2})-\log(1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{0})^{2})]

(7.24)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}((N\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}])^{-1}).

Remark.
Note that as long as
z
is separated from
\pm 2
by a constant
c
, then
|1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{2}(z)|\geq c^{\prime}
for some
c^{\prime}>0
.

Proof.
First, it is easy to see that,

(7.25)

\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\frac{1}{|\gamma_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}|^{4}}-\int\frac{1}{|x-\tilde{z}_{s}|^{4}}\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq\frac{C}{N\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]^{4}}.

Therefore,

(7.26)

\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]^{2}}{|\gamma_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}|^{4}}\mathrm{d}s-\int_{0}^{T}\int\frac{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]^{2}}{|x-\tilde{z}_{s}|^{4}}\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\right|\leq\frac{C}{N\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}]}.

The integral against
\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}
admits an exact calculation. First, observe that

(7.27)

2\int\frac{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]^{2}}{|x-\tilde{z}_{s}|^{4}}\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\mathrm{d}x=-\mathrm{Re}[m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(\tilde{z}_{s})]+\frac{\mathrm{Im}[m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{s})]}{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]}.

Since,

(7.28)

\partial_{s}\tilde{z}_{s}=-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{s})-\frac{\tilde{z}_{s}}{2}

we have

(7.29)

\frac{\mathrm{Im}[m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{s})]}{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]}=\frac{-\partial_{s}\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]}{\mathrm{Im}[z_{s}]}-\frac{1}{2}.

Next,

(7.30)

\partial_{s}\log(1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{2}(\tilde{z}_{s}))=\frac{-2m_{\mathrm{sc}}m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(\tilde{z}_{s})}{1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{2}(\tilde{z}_{s})}\partial_{s}\tilde{z}_{s}=-m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(\tilde{z}_{s})

where we used,

(7.31)

\partial_{s}\tilde{z}_{s}=-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{s})-\tilde{z}_{s}/2=\frac{1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{2}(\tilde{z}_{s})}{2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(\tilde{z}_{s})}

the second identity following from
m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{2}+zm_{\mathrm{sc}}+1=0
. Therefore,

(7.32)

2\int\frac{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]^{2}}{|x-\tilde{z}_{s}|^{4}}\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\mathrm{d}x=\partial_{s}\mathrm{Re}[\log(1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{2}(\tilde{z}_{s}))]-\frac{\partial_{s}\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]}{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]}-\frac{1}{2}.

The claim follows after integration in
s
. ∎

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
\tilde{z}_{s}
and
\tilde{w}_{s}
be two characteristics such that the final conditions satisfy
\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{z}_{T}],\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{w}_{T}]\in(-2+\kappa,2-\kappa)
and
\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}],\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{w}_{T}]\geq N^{\tau-1}
for some
\tau,\kappa>0
. Assume also that,

(7.33)

\kappa\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}]\leq\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{w}_{T}]\leq\frac{1}{\kappa}\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}].

Assume that

(7.34)

T<\frac{|\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{z}_{T}]-\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{w}_{T}]|}{\log(N)}.

Then,

(7.35)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{i}\frac{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{w}_{s}]}{|\gamma_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}|^{2}|\gamma_{i}-\tilde{w}_{s}|^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right|\leq C\frac{T^{2}+(\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}])^{2}}{(\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{z}_{T}]-\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{w}_{T}])^{2}}+C\frac{1}{N\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}]}

Proof.
Due to the explicit form of the characteristics, we see that

(7.36)

c\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]\leq\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{w}_{s}]\leq\frac{1}{c}\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}].

for some
c>0
for all
s\in[0,T]
.
Similar to the proof of the previous lemma we have,

(7.37)

\left|\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{i}\frac{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{w}_{s}]}{|\gamma_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}|^{2}|\gamma_{i}-\tilde{w}_{s}|^{2}}\mathrm{d}s-\int_{0}^{T}\int\frac{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{w}_{s}]}{|x-\tilde{z}_{s}|^{2}|x-\tilde{w}_{s}|^{2}}\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\right|\leq\frac{C}{N\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}]}.

The integral in
x
is bounded via convolution of two Poisson kernels,

(7.38)

\int\frac{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{w}_{s}]}{|x-\tilde{z}_{s}|^{2}|x-\tilde{w}_{s}|^{2}}\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\mathrm{d}x\leq C\frac{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]}{(\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{z}_{s}]-\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{w}_{s}])^{2}+(\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}])^{2}}.

Under our assumptions we have,

(7.39)

|\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{z}_{s}]-\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{w}_{s}]|\geq c|\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{z}_{T}]-\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{w}_{T}]|

for all
s\in[0,T]
, using the explicit form of the characteristics. Hence, using the explicit form of the characteristics to estimate
\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]\leq C(\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}]+T)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int\frac{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{s}]\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{w}_{s}]}{|x-\tilde{z}_{s}|^{2}|x-\tilde{w}_{s}|^{2}}\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}]+T}{|\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{z}_{T}]-\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{w}_{T}]|^{2}}\mathrm{d}s

(7.40)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\frac{T^{2}+(\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}])^{2}}{(\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{z}_{T}]-\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{w}_{T}])^{2}}

This yields the claim. ∎

The above two lemmas immediately imply the following.

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
\tilde{z}_{s}
and
\tilde{w}_{s}
be two characteristics such that the final conditions satisfy
\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{z}_{T}],\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{w}_{T}]\in(-2+\kappa,2-\kappa)
and
\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}],\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{w}_{T}]\geq N^{\tau-1}
for some
\tau,\kappa>0
. Assume that there is a
t=N^{-\omega}
such that

(7.41)

t\asymp\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}]\asymp\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{w}_{T}],

and a parameter
\delta>0
such that,

(7.42)

t\leq N^{-\delta}|\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{z}_{T}]-\mathrm{Re}[\tilde{w}_{T}]|.

Assume that there is
0<\delta_{1}<\delta
such that

(7.43)

T\asymp N^{\delta_{1}}t.

Then there is a
c>0
so that the variances of the Gaussian random variables

(7.44)

Z_{z}:=\frac{1}{N^{1/2}}\sum_{i}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{d}B_{i}}{\gamma_{i}-\tilde{z}_{s}}\mathrm{d}s,\qquad Z_{w}:=\frac{1}{N^{1/2}}\sum_{i}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{d}B_{i}}{\gamma_{i}-\tilde{w}_{s}}\mathrm{d}s

satisfy

(7.45)

\mathrm{Var}(Z_{z})\geq c\delta_{1}\log(N),\qquad\mathrm{Var}(Z_{w})\geq c\delta_{1}\log(N)

and their covariance satisfies

(7.46)

|\mathrm{Cov}(Z_{z},Z_{w})|\leq CN^{2(\delta_{1}-\delta)}+(Nt)^{-1}\leq\frac{C}{\log(N)}.

Proof.
The estimate for the covariance follows immediately. For the variance, note that
\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{0}]\asymp\mathrm{Im}[\tilde{z}_{T}]+T\asymp T
by the explicit form of the characteristics and the assumption that
T\gg t
. The lower bound follows. ∎

8
Estimates for the Gaussian ensembles

If
\rho_{k}
are the
k
-point functions of a point process (in our case the eigenvalue of a random matrix), i.e.,

(8.1)

\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[f(\lambda_{i})]=\int f(x)N\rho_{1}(x)\mathrm{d}x,\qquad\sum_{i\neq j}\mathbb{E}[f(\lambda_{i},\lambda_{j})]=N(N-1)\int f(x,y)\rho_{2}(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

then for two functions
f,g
we have

(8.2)

\mathrm{Cov}\left(\mathrm{tr}f(H),\mathrm{tr}g(H)\right)=\frac{1}{2}\int\left(f(x)-f(y)\right)\left(g(x)-g(y)\right)T_{N}(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

where the cluster function is,

(8.3)

T_{N}(x,y)=N^{2}\rho_{1}(x)\rho_{1}(y)-N(N-1)\rho_{2}(x,y).

Introduce the function,

(8.4)

g_{E,\eta}(x):=\frac{\eta}{(x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2}}

The un-normalized Hermite polynomials are

(8.5)

H_{n}(x)=(-1)^{n}\mathrm{e}^{x^{2}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\right)^{n}\mathrm{e}^{-x^{2}}.

They satisfy,

(8.6)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{e}^{-x^{2}}H_{n}(x)H_{m}(x)=\delta_{nm}2^{n}n!\sqrt{\pi}.

The normalized Hermite polyomials
h_{n}
are,

(8.7)

h_{n}(x):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}n!\sqrt{\pi}}}H_{n}(x)

which satisfy,

(8.8)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}h_{i}(x)h_{j}(x)\mathrm{e}^{-x^{2}}\mathrm{d}x=\delta_{ij}.

8.1
Covariance for GUE

With the above notation, define,

(8.9)

\psi_{n}(x):=\mathrm{e}^{-x^{2}/4}h_{n}(x/\sqrt{2})2^{-1/4}.

For the GUE, the Christoffel-Darboux formula is (see, e.g., Chapter 5 of
[
51
]
),

(8.10)

T_{N}(x,y)=N\left(\frac{\psi_{N}(\sqrt{N}x)\psi_{N-1}(\sqrt{N}y)-\psi_{N}(\sqrt{N}y)\psi_{N-1}(\sqrt{N}x)}{x-y}\right)^{2}=:\frac{\Psi_{N}(x,y)^{2}}{(x-y)^{2}}.

The estimates in Appendix
C
imply that,

(8.11)

|\Psi_{N}(x,y)|^{2}\leq C\left(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-cN(|x|-2)_{+}^{3/2}}}{|x-2|^{1/2}+N^{-1/3}}\right)\left(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-cN(|y|-2)_{+}^{3/2}}}{|y-2|^{1/2}+N^{-1/3}}\right)

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
0<\eta,\delta<10^{-1}
. There is a
C>0
depending on
\delta
but not on
\eta>0
so that the following holds. For any
E_{1},E_{2}
with
|E_{1}|,|E_{2}|<2-2\delta
we have,

(8.12)

\left|\mathrm{Cov}(\mathrm{tr}g_{E_{1},\eta}(H),\mathrm{tr}g_{E_{2},\eta}(H))\right|\leq C\frac{1}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}

Proof.
We see from the exponential tails in (
8.11
) that,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}(\mathrm{tr}g_{E_{1},\eta}(H),\mathrm{tr}g_{E_{2},\eta}(H))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{|x|,|y|<2+\delta}\frac{(g_{1}(x)-g_{1}(y))(g_{2}(x)-g_{2}(y))}{(x-y)^{2}}\Psi_{N}(x,y)^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.13)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\eta^{2}\mathrm{e}^{-cN}\right),

where we abbreviate
g_{i}=g_{E_{i},\eta}
. If
||x|-2|<\delta
and
|y|<2+\delta
it is not hard to see that,

(8.14)

\left|\frac{(g_{1}(x)-g_{1}(y))(g_{2}(x)-g_{2}(y))}{(x-y)^{2}}\right|\leq\frac{C\eta^{2}}{((y-E_{1})^{2}+\eta^{2})((y-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})}

Therefore,

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Cov}(\mathrm{tr}g_{1}(H),\mathrm{tr}g_{2}(H))|\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2}\int_{|x|,|y|<2-\delta}\frac{(g_{1}(x)-g_{1}(y))(g_{2}(x)-g_{2}(y))}{(x-y)^{2}}\Psi_{N}(x,y)^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|

(8.15)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C\frac{\eta}{((E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}

by the convolution property of Poisson kernels.
Now,

\displaystyle\frac{(g_{1}(x)-g_{1}(y))(g_{2}(x)-g_{2}(y))}{(x-y)^{2}}

(8.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\frac{(x-E_{1}+y-E_{1})(x-E_{2}+y-E_{2})}{((x-E_{1})^{2}+\eta^{2})((y-E_{1})^{2}+\eta^{2})((x-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})((y-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})}.

The claim then follows from Lemma
8.2
below and (
8.11
) above. ∎

Lemma 8.2
.

For
0\leq E<5
and
0<\eta<10^{-1}
we have,

(8.17)

\eta^{2}\int_{|x|,|y|<10}\frac{|(x+y)(x+y-2E)|}{(x^{2}+\eta^{2})(y^{2}+\eta^{2})((x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})((y-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\leq C\frac{1}{E^{2}+\eta^{2}}.

Proof.
We write the numerator as
(x+y)(x+y-2E)=x(x-E)+y(y-E)+x(y-E)+y(x-E)
. By symmetry it suffices to bound the
x(x-E)
term and the
x(y-E)
term. For the first we have,

\displaystyle\int_{|x|,|y|<10}\frac{|x(x-E)|}{(x^{2}+\eta^{2})(y^{2}+\eta^{2})((x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})((y-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\int_{|x|<10}\frac{|x(x-E)|}{(x^{2}+\eta^{2})((x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}x\right)\left(\int_{|y|<10}\frac{1}{(y^{2}+\eta^{2})((y-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}y\right).

The
y
integration is bounded above by
\eta^{-1}(E^{2}+\eta^{2})^{-1}
by the convolution property of the Poisson kernel. The integrand in the
x
integral can be bounded by
(x^{2}+\eta^{2})^{-1}+((x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})^{-1}
by Cauchy-Schwarz, and so yields
\mathcal{O}(\eta^{-1})
.

We now estimate the integral corresponding to
|x(y-E)|
. We have,

\displaystyle\int_{|x|<10}\frac{|x|}{(x^{2}+\eta^{2})((x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{|x|<E/2}\frac{|x|}{(x^{2}+\eta^{2})((x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\int_{2E>|x|>E/2}\frac{|x|}{(x^{2}+\eta^{2})((x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}x

(8.19)

\displaystyle+\int_{10>|x|>2E}\frac{|x|}{(x^{2}+\eta^{2})((x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}x.

The last integral is bounded by,

(8.20)

\int_{|x|>2E}\frac{|x|}{(x^{2}+\eta^{2})((x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}x\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{x^{3}+(E+\eta)^{3}}\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{C}{E^{2}+\eta^{2}}.

The second last integral is bounded by,

(8.21)

\int_{2E>|x|>E/2}\frac{|x|}{(x^{2}+\eta^{2})((x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{CE}{E^{2}+\eta^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{(x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2}}\mathrm{d}x\leq C\frac{E}{\eta(E^{2}+\eta^{2})}.

The first integral is bounded by,

(8.22)

\int_{|x|<E/2}\frac{|x|}{(x^{2}+\eta^{2})((x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}x\leq C\frac{|E|}{E^{2}+\eta^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{x^{2}+\eta^{2}}\mathrm{d}x\leq C\frac{E}{\eta(E^{2}+\eta^{2})}.

Therefore,

(8.23)

\int_{|x|<10}\frac{|x|}{(x^{2}+\eta^{2})((x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}x\leq C\frac{1}{\eta(E+\eta)}.

By making the change of variable
x=y-E
we see by the same argument as above,

(8.24)

\int_{|y|<10}\frac{|y-E|}{(y^{2}+\eta^{2})((y-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}y\leq C\frac{1}{\eta(E+\eta)}.

This completes the proof. ∎

8.2
GOE preliminaries

We first make some simplifications that will allow us to avoid considering the behavior of the cluster function near the spectral edges. Write
R_{N}(x,y)
for twice the cluster function of the GOE, i.e.,

(8.25)

\frac{1}{2}R_{N}(x,y)=N^{2}p_{1}(x)p_{1}(y)-N(N-1)p_{2}(x,y)

Let now
g_{i}
be as in the GUE case above, i.e., a Poisson kernel centered at
|E_{i}|\leq 2-6\delta
, some
\delta>0
. Let
\chi
be a bump function that is
1
on
[-2+4\delta,2-4\delta]
and
0
outside of
[-2+3\delta,2-3\delta]
. Define
f_{i}=\chi g_{i}
.

Let
\chi_{1}
be a bump function that is
0
outside of
[-2+\delta,2-\delta]
and
1
on
[-2+2\delta,2-2\delta]
.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
D>0
. Then, for
E_{i}
,
f_{i}
as
0<\eta<1
as above we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Cov}(\mathrm{tr}g_{1}(H),\mathrm{tr}g_{2}(H))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)R_{N}(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|

(8.26)

\displaystyle+CN^{\varepsilon}\frac{1}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}+\eta^{-2}N^{-D}.

Proof.
We have, (writing
\mathrm{tr}h(H)=h
for notational simplicity),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}(g_{1},g_{2})=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}(\chi g_{1},\chi g_{2})

(8.27)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}((1-\chi)g_{1},g_{2})+\mathrm{Cov}(g_{1},(1-\chi)g_{2})-\mathrm{Cov}((1-\chi)g_{1},(1-\chi)g_{2}).

Since
(1-\chi)g_{i}
equals
\eta
times a smooth function having derivatives bounded uniformly in
N
it is straightforward to conclude by Theorem
6.9
or rigidity that,

(8.28)

\mathrm{Var}((1-\chi)g_{i})\leq C\eta^{2}N^{\varepsilon}.

Moreover, since the function
\eta\to\eta\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(x+\mathrm{i}\eta)]
is increasing, it follows that

(8.29)

\mathrm{Var}(g_{i})\leq\eta^{-2}\mathbb{E}[N^{\varepsilon}\mathrm{Im}[m_{N}(E_{i}+\mathrm{i}N^{\varepsilon-1})]^{2}]\leq CN^{3\varepsilon}\eta^{-2},

where the last inequality follows by the local law (
2.11
).
It follows by Cauchy-Schwarz that,

(8.30)

|\mathrm{Cov}(g_{1},g_{2})|\leq|\mathrm{Cov}(f_{1},f_{2})|+CN^{\varepsilon}

where we defined
f_{i}=\chi g_{i}
.

We now show that we can restrict the domain of integration in the formula for the covariance of the
f_{i}
’s. Let
\chi_{1}
be a bump function as defined before the statement of the lemma that is
0
outside of
[-2+\delta,2-\delta]
and
1
on
[-2+2\delta,2-2\delta]
. Recall that
R_{N}
is symmetric and so,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}(\mathrm{tr}f_{1}(H),\mathrm{tr}f_{2}(H))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))R_{N}(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.31)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f_{1}(x)f_{2}(x)R_{N}(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f_{1}(x)f_{2}(y)R_{N}(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y.

On the other hand, consider,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)R_{N}(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f_{1}(x)f_{2}(x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)R_{N}(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.32)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f_{1}(x)f_{2}(y)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)R_{N}(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y.

Since the
f_{i}
are supported in
[-2+3\delta,2-3\delta]
and
\chi_{1}
is
1
on this interval, we see that
f_{1}(x)f_{2}(y)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)=f_{1}(x)f_{2}(y)
and
f_{1}(x)f_{2}(x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)=f_{1}(x)f_{2}(x)\chi_{1}(y)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}(\mathrm{tr}f_{1}(H),\mathrm{tr}f_{2}(H))-\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)R_{N}(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f_{1}(x)f_{2}(x)(1-\chi_{1}(y))R_{N}(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y.

Recall that
R_{N}(x,y)=2(N^{2}p_{1}(x)p_{1}(y)-N(N-1)p_{2}(x,y))
and so, letting
F(x)=f_{1}(x)f_{2}(x)
and
G(y)=1-\chi_{1}(y)
we have,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f_{1}(x)f_{2}(x)(1-\chi_{1}(y))R_{N}(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j}\mathbb{E}[F(\lambda_{i})G(\lambda_{j})]-\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}[F(\lambda_{i})]\mathbb{E}[G(\lambda_{j})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}[F(\lambda_{i})G(\lambda_{j})]-\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}[F(\lambda_{i})]\mathbb{E}[G(\lambda_{j})]

(8.34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}\left(\mathrm{tr}F(H),\mathrm{tr}G(H)\right)

where in the second equality we used that since
F
and
G
have disjoint support,
F(\lambda_{i})G(\lambda_{i})=0
. To bound the last term we treat the cases of
\eta\lesssim N^{-1}
and
\eta\gtrsim N^{-1}
separately.

Let
\varepsilon>0
, and consider
\eta\leq N^{\varepsilon-1}
. By rigidity we have with overwhelming probability that
\mathrm{tr}G(H)-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}G(H)]=\mathcal{O}(N^{\varepsilon})
. Therefore, (using that
F
is positive),

(8.35)

|\mathrm{Cov}\left(\mathrm{tr}F(H),\mathrm{tr}G(H)\right)|=|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}F(H)(\mathrm{tr}G(H)-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}G(H)])]|\leq N^{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}F(H)]+CN^{-D}\eta^{-2}

for any
D>0
. On the other hand, since the support of
F
is restricted to the bulk, we have that, (since the 1-point function or density of states of the GOE is bounded in the bulk, see Theorem
2.14
)

(8.36)

\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}F(H)]\leq CN\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{1}(x)f_{2}(x)\mathrm{d}x\leq CN\frac{\eta}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}\leq\frac{CN^{\varepsilon}}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}

where we used the convolution property for Poisson kernels as well as the fact that we are assuming
N\eta\leq N^{\varepsilon}
.

Assume now that
\eta\geq N^{\varepsilon-1}
. Then a straightforward calculation using rigidity shows that with overwhelming probability,

(8.37)

|\mathrm{tr}F(H)-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}F(H)]|\leq CN^{\varepsilon}\|F^{\prime}\|_{1}\leq C\frac{N^{\varepsilon}}{\eta}\|F\|_{1}\leq CN^{\varepsilon}\frac{1}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}},

again using the convolution property of the Poisson kernels.
This yields the claim. ∎

8.3
Covariance for GOE,
N
even

In this section, we prove the following.

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
g_{E,\eta}
be as in (
8.4
). Let
\kappa>0
and
\mathfrak{c}>0
and assume that
|E_{1}|,|E_{2}|\leq 2-\kappa
. Let
\varepsilon>0
. For any
N^{-\mathfrak{c}}<\eta<\frac{1}{2}
we have,

(8.38)

\left|\mathrm{Cov}\left(\mathrm{tr}g_{E_{1},\eta}(H),\mathrm{tr}g_{E_{2},\eta}(H)\right)\right|\leq CN^{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{|E_{1}-E_{2}|}{\eta((E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})}+\frac{1}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}\right)

where
H
is a GOE matrix of even dimension.

In order to prove the above Proposition, we have by Lemma
8.3
that it suffices to estimate the first term on the right side of (
8.26
). We now state the exact formula for the cluster function found in
[
51
]
.
With
\psi_{n}
as in (
8.9
), introduce

(8.39)

\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(x):=N^{1/4}\psi_{N}(\sqrt{N}x),\qquad\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(x):=N^{1/4}\psi_{N-1}(\sqrt{N}x)

By the asymptotics in Appendix
C
, we have

(8.40)

|\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(x)|+|\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(x)|\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-cN(|x|-2)_{+}^{3/2}}}{||x|-2|^{1/4}+N^{-1/6}}

Define the operator, for
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
,

(8.41)

\mathrm{sg}(x):=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{sign}(x),\qquad(\mathrm{sg}f)(t):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{sg}(t-s)f(s)\mathrm{d}s.

Note that for sufficiently well-behaved
f
we have,

(8.42)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathrm{sg}f=f.

From (6.1.19) and Theorem 6.1.6 and (6.2.10) of
[
51
]
we have for the cluster function defined as in (
8.25
) and
N
even,

\displaystyle R_{N}(x,y)=

\displaystyle 2S_{N}(x,y)S_{N}(y,x)

(8.43)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle D_{N}(x,y)(I_{N}(y,x)-\mathrm{sg}(y-x))+D_{N}(y,x)(I_{N}(x,y)-\mathrm{sg}(x-y))

and

(8.44)

\displaystyle S_{N}(x,y)=K_{N}(x,y)+\frac{N}{2}\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(x)\mathrm{sg}\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(y)

where

(8.45)

\displaystyle K_{N}(x,y)=\frac{\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(x)\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(y)-\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(y)\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(x)}{(x-y)}

(see
[
51
, (4.2.17), (5.1.3)]
for the Christoffel-Darboux formula in this scaling)
and,

(8.46)

\displaystyle D_{N}(x,y)=-\partial_{y}S_{N}(x,y),\qquad I_{N}(x,y)=\int_{y}^{x}S_{N}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s.

We apply the exact formula above, and decompose the covariance of
f_{i}
into several terms corresponding to the various terms in
R_{N}
above. To facilitate this we further introduce,

(8.47)

E_{N,1}(x,y)=\frac{N}{2}\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(x)\mathrm{sg}\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(y),\qquad\tilde{I}_{N}(x,y)=\int_{y}^{x}K_{N}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s

and

(8.48)

F_{N,1}(x,y)=\int_{y}^{x}E_{N,1}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s.

Importantly, note that

(8.49)

\partial_{x}\tilde{I}_{N}(x,y)=K_{N}(x,y),\qquad\partial_{x}F_{N,1}(x,y)=E_{N,1}(x,y).

Proof of Proposition
8.4
. By Lemma
8.3
it suffices to estimate the first term on the right side of (
8.26
).
After changing switching the roles of
x
and
y
in a few terms we find the following formula for the first term on the RHS of (
8.26
).

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))R_{N}(x,y)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.50)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))K_{N}(x,y)^{2}\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.51)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))(\partial_{y}K_{N}(x,y))\tilde{I}_{N}(y,x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.52)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))E_{N,1}(x,y)K_{N}(y,x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.53)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))E_{N,1}(x,y)E_{N,1}(y,x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.54)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))(\partial_{y}E_{N,1}(x,y))\tilde{I}_{N}(y,x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.55)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))(\partial_{y}K_{N}(x,y))F_{N,1}(y,x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.56)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))(\partial_{y}E_{N,1}(x,y))F_{N,1}(y,x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.57)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))(\partial_{y}S_{N}(x,y))\mathrm{sg}(y-x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

Over the next sequence of lemmas, we bound each of the terms appearing above, which will conclude the Proposition. ∎

Lemma 8.5
.

For the term (
8.50
) we have,

(8.58)

\displaystyle\left|\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))K_{N}(x,y)^{2}\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|\leq\frac{C}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}

Proof.
The region of integration is restricted to
|x|,|y|\leq 2-\delta
on which we have,

(8.59)

|K_{N}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C}{|x-y|},

which follows from the explicit form of
K_{N}
above and the Hermite polynomial asymptotics in Appendix
C
.
When
|x|,|y|<2-4\delta
we have that
f_{i}=g_{i}
and so by the proof of Proposition
8.1
(using Lemma
8.2
) we easily see that,

(8.60)

\int_{|x|,|y|<2-4\delta}\left|\frac{(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))}{(x-y)^{2}}\right|\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\leq\frac{C}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}.

When
|x|,|y|>2-5\delta
we see from the explicit form of
f_{i}
that,

(8.61)

\left|\frac{(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))}{(x-y)^{2}}\right|\leq C\eta^{2},

and so this portion of the integral contributes
\mathcal{O}(1)
. This leaves the cross-terms where
|x|>2-4\delta
and
|y|<2-5\delta
, or vice versa. By symmetry, it suffices to deal with the former region. When
|x|>2-4\delta
and
|y|<2-5\delta
we have that,

(8.62)

\left|\frac{(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))}{(x-y)^{2}}\right|\leq\frac{C\eta^{2}}{(y-E_{1})^{2}+\eta^{2}}\frac{1}{(y-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}

and so this region contributes
\mathcal{O}(\eta/((E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})
. The claim follows. ∎

Lemma 8.6
.

We have for
|x|,|y|<2-\delta
that

(8.63)

|\tilde{I}_{N}(x,y)|\leq C\log(N)(1\wedge(N|x-y|)).

Proof.
We may assume
x>y
; the proof for
x<y
is similar. We write, for some
y<A<x
,

(8.64)

\tilde{I}_{N}(x,y)=\int_{y}^{A}K_{N}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{A}^{x}K_{N}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s.

By (
8.59
) we have,

(8.65)

\left|\int_{A}^{x}K_{N}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s\right|\leq C\log\left(\frac{x-y}{A-y}\right).

For the other term we have by the explicit form of
K_{N}
and the fact that the
\varphi_{N}^{(N)}
and
\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}
are bounded in the domain of integration,

(8.66)

\left|\int_{y}^{A}K_{N}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s\right|\leq C\int_{y}^{A}\frac{|\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(s)-\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(y)|+|\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(s)-\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(y)|}{s-y}\mathrm{d}s.

By
[
51
, (5.2.4)]
, we have that
|\partial_{s}\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(s)|+|\partial_{s}\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(s)|\leq CN
for
|s|<2-\delta
and so this integral is bounded by
CN|A-y|
. If
|x-y|\leq N^{-1}
we take
A=x
. If
|x-y|\geq N^{-1}
we take
A=y+N^{-1}
. The estimate follows. ∎

Lemma 8.7
.

For the term (
8.51
) we have,

\displaystyle\left|\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))(\partial_{y}K_{N}(x,y))\tilde{I}_{N}(y,x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|

(8.67)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\log(N)\left(\frac{|E_{1}-E_{2}|}{\eta((E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})}+\frac{1}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}\right)

Proof.
We will treat the integral by integrating by parts in
y
. Note that the integrand is of compact support so there is no boundary term. For the term arising from the partial integration where the derivative hits
\tilde{I}_{N}
we have,
\partial_{y}\tilde{I}_{N}(y,x)=K_{N}(y,x)
, and so this term was dealt with in Lemma
8.5
. Consider the term where where the partial derivative hits
\chi_{1}(y)
. Note that

(8.68)

(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))\chi_{1}^{\prime}(y)=f_{1}(x)f_{2}(x)\chi_{1}^{\prime}(y)

and that this term is non-zero only if
|x-y|\geq\delta
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))\chi_{1}^{\prime}(y)\chi_{1}(x)K_{N}(x,y)\tilde{I}_{N}(x,y)|\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.69)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\log(N)\int_{\mathbb{R}}|f_{1}(x)f_{2}(x)\chi_{1}(x)|\mathrm{d}x\leq C\log(N)\frac{\eta}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}.

It remains to consider the term,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|(\partial_{y}[(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))])K_{N}(x,y)\tilde{I}_{N}(y,x)|\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.70)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\log(N)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\frac{\partial_{y}[(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))]}{x-y}\right|\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

where we used (
8.59
) and (
8.63
).
If both
|x|,|y|>2-5\delta
then,

(8.71)

\left|\frac{\partial_{y}[(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))]}{x-y}\right|\leq C\eta^{2}

by the explicit form of the
f_{i}
. If
|x|>2-4\delta
and
|y|<2-5\delta
then one can estimate easily,

(8.72)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial_{y}[(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))]}{x-y}\right|\leq C\frac{\eta}{((y-E_{1})^{2}+\eta^{2})((y-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})}.

The integral over
x
and
y
contributes an acceptable error by the convolution property of Poisson kernels. For the region
|x|<2-5\delta
and
|y|>2-4\delta
we have,

(8.73)

\left|\frac{\partial_{y}[(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))]}{x-y}\right|\leq C\frac{\eta^{2}}{((x-E_{1})^{2}+\eta^{2})((x-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})}

and we obtain a similar (in fact, better) estimate. For
|x|,|y|<2-4\delta
we have that
f_{i}=g_{i}
. By symmetry it will suffice to consider the case that the derivative hits
g_{1}(y)
. In this case, we can estimate,

(8.74)

\displaystyle\left|(\partial_{y}g_{1}(y))\frac{g_{2}(x)-g_{2}(y)}{(x-y)}\right|\leq C\eta^{2}\frac{|y-E_{1}|}{(y-E_{1})^{4}+\eta^{4}}\frac{|x-E_{2}|+|y-E_{2}|}{((x-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})((y-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})}.

By estimating the integral of the right side over
|x|,|y|<10
it suffices to check the case
E_{1}=0
and
E_{2}=E
for some
0<E<5
. There are two terms, corresponding to the two terms
|x-E_{2}|
and
|y-E_{2}|
on the right side above. The
|x-E_{2}|
term is bounded by,

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\int_{|y|<10}\frac{|y|}{(y^{4}+\eta^{4})((y-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}y\int_{|x|<10}\frac{|x-E|}{(x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2}}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\log(N)\int_{|y|<10}\frac{\eta}{(y^{2}+\eta^{2})((y-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}y

(8.75)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\log(N)\frac{1}{E^{2}+\eta^{2}}.

In the first inequality we did the
x
integration which contributes
\mathcal{O}(|\log\eta|)=\mathcal{O}(\log(N))
and cleared a power of
y^{2}+\eta^{2}
in the denominator using the
|y\eta|
in the numerator. The last estimate follows from the convolution property of Poisson kernels.

The final term to consider is,

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\int_{|y|<10}\frac{|y||y-E|}{(y^{4}+\eta^{4})((y-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}y\int_{|x|<10}\frac{1}{(x-E)^{2}+\eta^{2}}\mathrm{d}x

(8.76)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\eta\int_{|y|<10}\frac{|y||y-E|}{(y^{4}+\eta^{4})((y-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}y

For this integral, first,

(8.77)

\eta\int_{|y|<E/2}\frac{|y||y-E|}{(y^{4}+\eta^{4})((y-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}y\leq C\frac{E}{E^{2}+\eta^{2}}\int\frac{\eta|y|}{y^{4}+\eta^{4}}\mathrm{d}y\leq C\frac{E}{\eta(E^{2}+\eta^{2})}.

Second,

(8.78)

\eta\int_{|y|>E/2}\frac{|y||y-E|}{(y^{4}+\eta^{4})((y-E)^{2}+\eta^{2})}\mathrm{d}y\leq\frac{C}{E^{2}+\eta^{2}}\int_{|y|<10}\frac{|y-E|}{(y-E)^{2}+\eta^{2}}\mathrm{d}y\leq\frac{C\log(N)}{E^{2}+\eta^{2}}.

Summarizing, we have shown

\displaystyle\int_{|x|,|y|<2-4\delta}\left|(\partial_{y}g_{1}(y))\frac{g_{2}(x)-g_{2}(y)}{(x-y)}\right|\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.79)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\log(N)\left(\frac{|E_{1}-E_{2}|}{\eta((E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})}+\frac{1}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}\right).

As stated above, the same argument applies to when the derivative hits
g_{2}
instead. This completes the proof. ∎

Lemma 8.8
.

We have, for
|x|,|y|<2-\delta
,

(8.80)

|E_{N,1}(x,y)|\leq C,\qquad|F_{N,1}(x,y)|\leq C.

Proof.
The estimate follows from
[
51
, (6.2.9)]
. ∎

Lemma 8.9
.

We have for the terms (
8.52
) and (
8.53
),

\displaystyle\left|\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))E_{N,1}(x,y)K_{N}(y,x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|

(8.81)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}

and

(8.82)

\displaystyle\left|\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))E_{N,1}(x,y)E_{N,1}(y,x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|\leq\frac{C}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}

Proof.
By Lemma
8.8
we have that
|E_{N,1}(x,y)|\leq C
for all
x
and
y
in the region where the integrand is non-zero. Therefore, we can crudely estimate,

(8.83)

|E_{N,1}(s,t)|\leq\frac{C}{|s-t|}.

The integral of
|(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))(x-y)^{-2}|
over
|x|,|y|<2-\delta
was estimated in the proof of Lemma
8.5
. ∎

Lemma 8.10
.

For the term (
8.54
) we have,

\displaystyle\left|\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))(\partial_{y}E_{N,1}(x,y))\tilde{I}_{N}(y,x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|

(8.84)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\log(N)\left(\frac{|E_{1}-E_{2}|}{\eta((E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})}+\frac{1}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}\right)

Proof.
We integrate by parts in
y
. There is no boundary term due to the compact support of the integrand. When this derivative hits
\tilde{I}_{N}(y,x)
one gets
K_{N}(y,x)
. This term was dealt with in Lemma
8.9
. When the derivative hits the
f_{i}
’s, one can, as in Lemma
8.9
simply bound
|E_{N,1}(s,t)|\leq C|s-t|^{-1}
and so one is left with estimating the integral of

(8.85)

\left|(\partial_{y}[(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y)])|x-y|^{-1}\tilde{I}_{N}(x,y)\right|

over
|x|,|y|<2-\delta
. This was completed in Lemma
8.7
. The final term is when the derivative hits
\chi_{1}(y)
, which is treated similarly to the analogous term in the proof of Lemma
8.7
. That is, due to the support properties of the
f_{i}
we have that the integrand is bounded by,

(8.86)

|(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))\chi_{1}^{\prime}(y)E_{N,1}(x,y)\tilde{I}_{N}(x,y)|\leq C\log(N)f_{1}(x)f_{2}(x)

which contributes an acceptable error after integration in
x
due the convolution property of the Poisson kernel.
∎

Lemma 8.11
.

For the terms (
8.55
) and (
8.56
) we have,

\displaystyle\left|\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))(\partial_{y}K_{N}(x,y))F_{N,1}(y,x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|

(8.87)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\log(N)\left(\frac{|E_{1}-E_{2}|}{\eta((E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})}+\frac{1}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}\right)

and

\displaystyle\left|\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))[\partial_{y}E_{N,1}(x,y)F_{N,1}(y,x)]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|

(8.88)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\log(N)\left(\frac{|E_{1}-E_{2}|}{\eta((E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})}+\frac{1}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}\right)

Proof.
We start with the first estimate. Partial integration in
y
leads to a term with
K_{N}(x,y)E_{N,1}(y,x)
as well as one where the derivative hits the
f_{i}
’s and
\chi_{1}
. The former term already appeared in Lemma
8.9
and was estimated there. The term where the derivative hits the
f_{i}
’s can be estimated in the same manner as Lemma
8.7
using the estimate of Lemma
8.8
for
F_{N,1}
(instead of the estimate
|\tilde{I}_{N}(x,y)|\leq C\log(N)
which was used in that proof). The integrand that arises when the derivative hits
\chi_{1}(y)
is bounded by
Cf_{1}(x)f_{2}(x)
which again contributes an acceptable error after integration in
x
.

For the second estimate stated in the lemma, we again integrate by parts. The term arising when the derivative hits
F_{N,1}
is
E_{N,1}(x,y)E_{N,1}(y,x)
, which was estimated in Lemma
8.9
. When the derivative hits the
f_{i}
’s we can proceed similarly to Lemma
8.7
. ∎

Lemma 8.12
.

For the term (
8.57
) we have,

\displaystyle\left|\int(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))[\partial_{y}S_{N}(x,y)\mathrm{sg}(y-x)]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right|

(8.89)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\log(N)\left(\frac{|E_{1}-E_{2}|}{\eta((E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2})}+\frac{1}{(E_{1}-E_{2})^{2}+\eta^{2}}\right)

Proof.
We again integrate by parts. Since we have
\mathrm{sg}(y-x)
there is a boundary term at
y=x
. However, this boundary term is
0
due to the fact that
S_{N}
is a continuous function, and the factors
(f_{i}(x)-f_{i}(y))
which vanish when
x=y
. There are then terms that arise when the derivative hits the
f_{i}
’s and when it hits
\chi_{1}
. Since
|S_{N}(x,y)|\leq C\log(N)|x-y|^{-1}
and
|\mathrm{sg}(y-x)|\leq 1
, these kinds of terms have been estimated in the previous lemmas. ∎

8.4
Covariance for GOE,
N
odd

In this section we prove the following.

Proposition 8.13
.

The conclusions of Proposition
8.4
hold also for GOE matrices of odd dimension.

We again use Lemma
8.3
and so in this section we estimate the first term on the right side of (
8.26
). The exact formula for
R_{N}(x,y)
in the case of odd dimension is similar to the case of even dimension, except for a few terms. After introducing the formulas, we just show how to handle the extra terms that arise, as the rest is the same as the case of even dimension.

The work
[
51
]
does not contain formulas for
N
odd, so we use Mehta’s book
[
48
]
. We will list the formulas there, rescale them to match our convention and then relate them to the quantities listed in the
N
-even case. We will need a few new estimates for some of the terms that appear.

The formulas in
[
48
]
are given in terms of the Hermite functions,

(8.90)

\varphi_{n}(x)=\frac{1}{(2^{n}n!\sqrt{\pi})^{1/2}}\mathrm{e}^{-x^{2}/2}H_{n}(x)=\mathrm{e}^{-x^{2}/2}h_{n}(x),

with
H_{n}
and
h_{n}
as in (
8.5
) and (
8.7
), and
are orthonormal. Compared with (
8.9
) we have,

(8.91)

\psi_{n}(x)=2^{-1/4}\varphi_{n}(x2^{-1/2}).

From
[
48
, (7.2.20)-(7.2.27)]
(which defines
\tilde{K}
below), and
[
48
, (7.2.31)]
(which defines the cluster function in terms of
\tilde{K}
, but see also
[
48
, (7.2.30)]
for the correlation functions as well as Chapter 5.1 for the definition of the determinant of quarternions, and the formula for the covariance in terms of correlation functions above) and
[
48
, (A.10.1)]
for the Christoffel-Darboux formula in this scaling, we have,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}(\mathrm{tr}f(H),\mathrm{tr}g(H))=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle(f(x(2/N)^{1/2})-f(y(2/N)^{1/2}))(g(x(2/N)^{1/2})-g(y(2/N)^{1/2}))

(8.92)

\displaystyle\times\mathrm{tr}\left(\tilde{K}_{N}(x,y)\tilde{K}_{N}(y,x)\right)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

where,

(8.93)

\displaystyle\tilde{K}_{N}(x,y):=\left(\begin{matrix}\tilde{S}_{N}(x,y)&\tilde{D}_{N}(x,y)\\
\tilde{J}_{N}(x,y)&\tilde{S}_{N}(y,x)\end{matrix}\right)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{N}(x,y)

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{N}{2}\right)^{1/2}\frac{\varphi_{N}(x)\varphi_{N-1}(y)-\varphi_{N}(y)\varphi_{N-1}(x)}{x-y}

\displaystyle+\varphi_{N-1}(x)\left(\left(\frac{N}{2}\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{sg}\varphi_{N}(y)+\frac{1}{\int\varphi_{N-1}(t)\mathrm{d}t}\right),

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{N}(x,y)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{\partial}{\partial y}\tilde{S}_{N}(x,y),

\displaystyle\tilde{J}_{N}(x,y)

\displaystyle:=\int\mathrm{sg}(x-t)\tilde{S}_{N}(t,y)\mathrm{d}t

(8.94)

\displaystyle-\mathrm{sg}(x-y)-\mathrm{sg}\varphi_{N-1}(y)\left(\int\varphi_{N-1}(t)\mathrm{d}t\right)^{-1}.

So, rescaling, we obtain

(8.95)

\mathrm{Cov}(\mathrm{tr}f(H),\mathrm{tr}g(H))=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(f(x)-f(y))(g(x)-g(y))\mathrm{tr}\hat{K}_{N}(x,y)\hat{K}_{N}(y,x)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

where

(8.96)

\hat{K}_{N}(x,y)=\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{S}_{N}(x,y)&(2/N)^{1/2}\hat{D}_{N}(x,y)\\
(N/2)^{1/2}\hat{J}_{N}(x,y)&\hat{S}_{N}(y,x)\end{matrix}\right)

and

\displaystyle\hat{S}_{N}(x,y)

\displaystyle:=K_{N}(x,y)+\hat{E}_{N,1}(x,y),

\displaystyle\hat{E}_{N,1}(x,y)

\displaystyle:=\frac{N}{2}\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(x)\mathrm{sg}\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(y)+\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(x)(\int\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(t)\mathrm{d}t)^{-1},

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{N}(x,y)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{\partial}{\partial y}\hat{S}_{N}(x,y),

\displaystyle\hat{J}_{N}(x,y)

\displaystyle:=\int\mathrm{sg}(x-t)\hat{S}_{N}(t,y)\mathrm{d}t

(8.97)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon(x-y)-\mathrm{sg}\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(y)\left(\int\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(t)\mathrm{d}t)\right)^{-1}.

So we have in this case that twice the cluster function is given by,

(8.98)

R_{N}(x,y)=2\hat{S}_{N}(x,y)\hat{S}_{N}(y,x)+\hat{J}_{N}(x,y)\hat{D}_{N}(y,x)+\hat{J}_{N}(y,x)\hat{D}_{N}(x,y).

We now prove some estimates on the terms arising above.

Lemma 8.14
.

Let
\delta>0
. For
x,y
satisfying
|x|,|y|<2-\delta
we have,

(8.99)

|\hat{E}_{N,1}(x,y)|\leq C.

Proof.
Let
N=2m+1
. By
[
51
, (6.2.8)]
we have,

(8.100)

|\hat{E}_{N,1}(x,y)-\hat{E}_{N,1}(x,0)|\leq C.

For the latter term, we have

(8.101)

\hat{E}_{N,1}(x,0)=\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(x)\left(\frac{N}{2}\mathrm{sg}\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(0)+(\int\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(t)\mathrm{d}t)^{-1}\right),

by definition. We now estimate the factor appearing on the RHS, showing it is
\mathcal{O}(1)
.
We have,

\displaystyle\frac{N}{2}\mathrm{sg}\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(0)

\displaystyle=-\frac{N}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=-\left(\frac{N}{2}\right)^{3/4}\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi_{2m+1}(t)\mathrm{d}t

(8.102)

\displaystyle=-\frac{m^{1/2}}{2^{1/2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(1\right).

In the first line we used the fact that
\varphi_{N}^{(N)}
is an odd function. In the second line we used the definition of
\varphi_{N}^{(N)}
in terms of the Hermite functions. In the last line we used Lemma
D.2
.
For the other term we have,

\displaystyle\int\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{N}{2}\right)^{-1/4}\int\varphi_{2m}(t)\mathrm{d}t

(8.103)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{1/2}}{m^{1/2}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1})\right)

We used Lemma
D.1
in the second line.
The claim follows after using the fact that the Hermite functions are bounded in the domain in question. ∎

Lemma 8.15
.

Let
\delta>0
. We have for
x,y
satisfying
|x|,|y|<2-\delta
that.

(8.104)

\left|\int\mathrm{sg}(x-t)\hat{E}_{N,1}(t,y)\right|\leq C

and

(8.105)

\left|\mathrm{sg}\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(y)\left(\int\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(t)\mathrm{d}t)\right)^{-1}\right|\leq C

Proof.
By
[
51
, (6.2.8)]
we have

(8.106)

|\mathrm{sg}\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(y)-\mathrm{sg}\varphi_{N}^{(N)}(0)|\leq CN^{-1}

and so it suffices to prove the first estimate with
y=0
due to the fact that scaled Hermite functions
\varphi_{N}^{(N)}
and
\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}
have bounded
L^{1}
norms. For
y=0
, we have the explicit form (
8.101
) for which the factor on the RHS is
\mathcal{O}(1)
, as shown in the previous proof. The first estimate follows.

For the second estimate, note that the first estimate of
[
51
, (6.2.9)]
(this applies since here
N
is odd and there
N
is even) implies that

|\mathrm{sg}\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}(y)|\leq CN^{-1}
. The previous proof shows that the other factor is
\mathcal{O}(N^{1/2})
and so we conclude the proof. ∎

Lemma 8.16
.

Let
|x|,|y|<2-\delta
. Then,

(8.107)

\left|\int\mathrm{sg}(x-t)\tilde{S}_{N}(t,y)\mathrm{d}t\right|\leq C\log(N),

and so,

(8.108)

|\hat{J}_{N}(x,y)|\leq C\log(N).

Proof.
First,

(8.109)

\int_{t:|t-y|>\delta/2}|\tilde{S}_{N}(t,y)|\mathrm{d}t\leq C(\|\varphi_{N}^{(N)}\|_{1}+\|\varphi_{N-1}^{(N)}\|_{1})\leq C,

by the asymptotics (
8.40
). Next, the proof of Lemma
8.6
shows that,

(8.110)

\int_{t:|t-y|\leq\delta/2}|K_{N}(t,y)|\mathrm{d}t\leq C\log(N),

and the claim follows. ∎

Proof of Proposition
8.13
.
If suffices to estimate the first term on the right side of (
8.26
). For this term we see from the above that we have the formula,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)R_{N}(x,y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.111)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))\hat{S}_{N}(x,y)\hat{S}_{N}(y,x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y

(8.112)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))(\partial_{y}\hat{S}_{N}(x,y))\hat{J}_{N}(y,x)\chi_{1}(x)\chi_{1}(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y.

Given the estimates that we have established above, estimating the above terms is very similar to the proof of Proposition
8.4
. Given Lemma
8.14
, the term (
8.111
) is estimated in the exact same fashion as the terms (
8.50
), (
8.52
) and (
8.53
). For the term (
8.112
), we integrate by parts in
y
. When the derivative hits
\hat{J}_{N}(y,x)
we use
\partial_{y}\hat{J}_{N}(y,x)=\hat{S}_{N}(y,x)
and see that this term is identical to (
8.111
). When the derivative hits the other terms we see from Lemma
8.16
that the integrand is bounded by

(8.113)

C\log(N)^{2}|(\partial_{y}[\chi_{1}(y)(f_{1}(y)-f_{1}(x))(f_{2}(x)-f_{2}(y))])(x-y)^{-1}|.

Estimation of the integral of this quantity was handled in the proof of Lemma
8.7
. ∎

8.5
Expectation

Lemma 8.17
.

Let
f
be a function, and
H
a matrix from the GOE. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}f(H)]

\displaystyle=N\int f(x)\rho_{\mathrm{sc}}(x)\mathrm{d}x-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int f(x)\frac{1}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x

(8.114)

\displaystyle+\frac{f(2)+f(-2)}{4}+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w})

Proof.
This follows immediately from Lemma
6.11
.
∎

9
Proof of main theorems

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
\varphi\in H^{1/2+s}
for
s>0
. Let
H
be a Gaussian divisible ensemble of the form

(9.1)

H=\mathrm{e}^{-T/2}W+\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-T}}G

where
T=N^{-\omega}
and
\omega>0
satisfies,

(9.2)

\omega<\frac{s}{100}.

Then,

(9.3)

\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}(\varphi(H)))\leq C\|\varphi\|_{H^{1/2+s}}^{2}.

Proof.
Fix a small
\mathfrak{a}>0
. Let
\chi
be a smooth bump function that is 1 on the interval
[-1,1]
and
0
outside of
[-2,2]
. Define

(9.4)

\hat{\psi}_{1}(\xi)=\chi(\xi/N^{\mathfrak{a}})\hat{\varphi}(\xi),

and

(9.5)

\hat{\psi}_{2}(\xi)=(1-\chi(\xi/N^{\mathfrak{a}}))\sum_{|k-\mathfrak{a}\log_{2}(N)|<100}\hat{\varphi}_{k}(\xi)

and

(9.6)

\hat{\psi}_{3}(\xi)=\sum_{k\geq 50+\mathfrak{a}\log_{2}(N)}\hat{\varphi}_{k}(\xi).

By the properties of the Littlewood-Paley decomposition,

(9.7)

\varphi=\psi_{1}+\psi_{2}+\psi_{3}.

By
[
3
, Lemma 2.1, p. 52]
, we have that

(9.8)

\|\psi_{1}\|_{C^{2}}+\|\psi_{2}\|_{C^{2}}\leq CN^{3\mathfrak{a}/2}\|\varphi\|_{2}.

We choose
\mathfrak{a}=1/10
. Now, if
p
is the inverse Fourier transform of
\chi
we have,

(9.9)

\psi_{1}(x)=N^{\mathfrak{a}}\int p(N^{\mathfrak{a}}(x-y))\varphi(y)\mathrm{d}x.

If
x\notin(-2+\kappa/2,2-\kappa/2)
we see from the fact that,

(9.10)

|p^{(m)}(x)|\leq\frac{C_{n,m}}{1+|x|^{n}}

that,

(9.11)

|\psi^{(m)}_{1}(x)|\leq CN^{-100}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{10}}\|\varphi\|_{2}.

for
0\leq m\leq 5
and
x\notin(-2+\kappa/2,2-\kappa/2)
. By the properties of the Littlewood-Paley decomposition, we see that

(9.12)

\hat{\psi}_{2}(\xi)=(1-\chi(\xi/N^{\mathfrak{a}}))q(\xi/N^{\mathfrak{a}})\hat{\varphi}(\xi)

for some smooth
q
that is of compact support. Explicitly,

(9.13)

q(\xi)=\sum_{|k|\leq 100}\hat{\omega}(2^{-k}\xi).

By the same argument as above, we see that

(9.14)

|\psi^{(m)}_{2}(x)|\leq CN^{-100}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{10}}\|\varphi\|_{2}

for
0\leq m\leq 5
and
x\notin(-2+\kappa/2,2-\kappa/2)
as well.
Therefore by Theorem
6.9
we have that,

(9.15)

|\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}\psi_{1}(H))-V(\psi_{1})|+|\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}\psi_{2}(H))-V(\psi_{2})|\leq N^{-1}N^{3\mathfrak{a}+\varepsilon}\|\varphi\|_{2}^{2}.

Clearly

(9.16)

\|\psi_{1}\|_{2}+\|\psi_{2}\|_{2}\leq C\|\varphi\|_{2}.

We see from the above estimates that

(9.17)

|V(\psi_{i})|\leq C\|\varphi\|_{2}^{2}+C\int_{(-2+\kappa/4,2-\kappa/4)^{2}}\frac{(\psi_{i}(x)-\psi_{i}(y))^{2}}{(x-y)^{2}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y.

The last term is bounded above by the integral over
\mathbb{R}^{2}
which is, up to constants, the norm
\|\psi_{i}\|_{\dot{H}^{1/2}}
. Clearly,

(9.18)

\|\psi_{i}\|_{\dot{H}^{1/2}}\leq C\|\varphi\|_{H^{1/2+s}}

and so

(9.19)

\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}(\psi_{1}(H)))+\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}(\psi_{2}(H)))\leq C\|\varphi\|_{H^{1/2+s}}.

Let

(9.20)

M=10s^{-1}\log_{2}(N).

and

(9.21)

\psi_{4}:=\sum_{k\geq M}\varphi_{k}.

By Theorem
3.5
we have,

(9.22)

\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}(\psi_{4}(H)))\leq CN^{-1}\|\varphi\|_{H^{1/2+s}}.

Consider now
k
such that

(9.23)

\mathfrak{a}\log_{2}(N)-100\leq k\leq M.

By Theorems
3.3
and
3.4
we have,

(9.24)

\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}\varphi_{k}(H))\leq CN^{\varepsilon+\omega}2^{k}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}+N^{-100}\|\varphi\|_{2}^{2}

for any
\varepsilon>0
. We may now choose
\varepsilon>0
so that,

(9.25)

\varepsilon+\omega<\frac{\mathfrak{a}s}{2}.

Then

(9.26)

N^{\varepsilon+\omega}2^{k}\|\varphi_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\leq N^{-\mathfrak{a}s}\|\varphi\|^{2}_{H^{1/2+s}}.

Hence

(9.27)

\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}(\psi_{3}-\psi_{4})(H))\leq C\log(N)N^{-\mathfrak{a}s}\|\varphi\|^{2}_{H^{1/2+s}}.

The claim follows.∎

9.1
Proof of Theorem
1.4

We recall the following,
[
27
, Proposition 4.1]
.

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
u
be a Wigner-smooth probability density as in Definition
1.2
. For any
K>0
there is an
\alpha_{K}>0
so that for any positive
t<\alpha_{K}
there exists a probability density
p_{t}
with mean
0
and variance
1
so that

(9.28)

\int|\mathrm{e}^{tA}p_{t}-u|\mathrm{d}x\leq Ct^{K},

where
A=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}^{2}-\frac{x}{2}\partial-x
is the generator of the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck flow. Moreover,
g_{t}
is Wigner-smooth with uniform constants depending on those for
u
.

The only difference from the above and the construction in
[
27
]
is the slightly weaker assumption on the decay, but that this holds can be checked by the explicit form of the short construction given there.

Now consider a smooth Wigner matrix
H
and a function
\varphi\in H^{1/2+s}
. Denote the density of its off-diagonal entries by
u_{0}
and of the diagonal entries by
u_{d}
. Let,

(9.29)

M=10s^{-1}\log_{2}(N),

and

(9.30)

T=N^{-\omega},\qquad\omega=\frac{s}{100}.

Let,

(9.31)

\psi_{1}=\sum_{k\geq-1}^{M}\varphi_{k},\qquad\psi_{2}=\sum_{k>M}\varphi_{k}.

We see from Theorem
3.5
that

(9.32)

\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{tr}\psi_{2}(H))\leq CN^{-1}\|\varphi\|_{H^{1/2+s}}^{2}.

We choose
K
sufficiently large so that with
t=T
, Proposition
9.2
guarantees existence of a densities
p_{o},p_{d}
so that,

(9.33)

\int|\mathrm{e}^{tA}p_{o}-u_{o}|\mathrm{d}x+\int|\mathrm{e}^{tA}p_{d}-u_{d}|\mathrm{d}x\leq C\frac{1}{N^{100}}\frac{1}{2^{10M}}.

Let
W_{1}
be the Wigner ensemble with off-diagonal densities
p_{0}
and diagonal densities
p_{d}
and
W=\mathrm{e}^{-t/2}W_{1}+\sqrt{1-\mathrm{e}^{-t}}G
with
G
GOE so that
W
has densities
\mathrm{e}^{tA}p_{d}
and
\mathrm{e}^{tA}p_{o}
. We apply Theorem
9.1
to
W
.
1
†
†
footnotetext:
1
. Note that strictly speaking, the third and fourth cumulants of
W_{1}
may not satisfy (
1.19
) but, by (
9.33
), and since
H
does satisfy (
1.19
),
W_{1}
satisfies it up to, say, an error of
\mathcal{O}(N^{-100})
which does not affect the proof.

For
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\geq 2^{-M}
it follows from the Lipschitz continuity of the resolvent that,

\displaystyle\bigg|

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}\left(\mathrm{Im}\left[\mathrm{tr}(H-x-\mathrm{i}\eta_{1})^{-1}\right],\mathrm{Im}\left[\mathrm{tr}(H-y-\mathrm{i}\eta_{2})^{-1}\right]\right)

(9.34)

\displaystyle-\mathrm{Cov}\left(\mathrm{Im}\left[\mathrm{tr}(W-x-\mathrm{i}\eta_{1})^{-1}\right],\mathrm{Im}\left[\mathrm{tr}(W-y-\mathrm{i}\eta_{2})^{-1}\right]\right)\bigg|\leq CN^{-10}.

From the decomposition,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(\sum_{k\leq M}\mathrm{tr}\varphi_{k}(H))

(9.35)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l,k\leq M}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}g_{l}(x)g_{k}(y)\mathrm{Cov}\left(\mathrm{Im}\left[\mathrm{tr}(H-x-\mathrm{i}\eta_{l})^{-1}\right],\mathrm{Im}\left[\mathrm{tr}(H-y-\mathrm{i}\eta_{k})^{-1}\right]\right)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y,

the estimate

(9.36)

\left|\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{k\leq M}\mathrm{tr}\varphi_{k}(H)\right)-\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{k\leq M}\mathrm{tr}\varphi_{k}(H)\right)\right|\leq N^{-2}\|\varphi\|^{2}_{2}

follows. This yields the estimate for functions in
H^{1/2+s}
. ∎

10
Fine estimates of characteristic functions

Let
f
be a smooth function supported in
[-5,5]
and
H
a Wigner matrix. Recall the quasi-analytic extension of
f
given by,

(10.1)

\tilde{f}(x+\mathrm{i}y):=(f(x)+\mathrm{i}yf^{\prime}(x))\chi(y)

where
\chi
is an even bump function that is
1
for
|y|<1
and
0
for
|y|>2
.
We fix
\mathfrak{a}>0
for the rest of the section and define

(10.2)

e_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi):=\exp\left[\mathrm{i}\xi\left(\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{f}(z)N(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right)\right],

and

(10.3)

\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi):=\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi)],

where
\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}
was defined in (
6.80
). Lemma
6.8
implies that
\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}
is a good approximation for the characteristic function of the linear statistic.

10.1
Self-consistent equation for Stein’s method

We will apply Stein’s method to calculate
\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi)
. The main tool technical estimate that we will use in our application of Stein’s method is the following proposition.

Proposition 10.1
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
\tau>0
. For any
z=x+\mathrm{i}y
with
z\in\mathcal{D}_{\tau,1}
we have,

\displaystyle(z+2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))N\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi)(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\mathrm{i}\xi}{N}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\psi_{a}\partial_{w}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}\frac{4s_{3}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)\mathrm{i}\xi}{\pi N^{1/2}}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)(m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)+m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w))m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{2\xi^{2}s_{3}}{\pi^{2}N^{1/2}}\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{-2\xi\mathrm{i}s_{4}}{\pi}\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

(10.4)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}(1+|\xi|^{6})(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1}^{6})\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1})y^{-1}+N^{-1}y^{-2})

Above,
w=u+\mathrm{i}v
.

The proof of the above proposition is largely similar to the proof of Proposition
6.1
, in that it will be seen to be a consequence of
a cumulant expansion. First, we have that whenever the entry-wise local semicircle law holds,

(10.5)

|\partial_{ia}^{k}e_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi)|\leq C(1+|\xi|)^{k}N^{\varepsilon}(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1})^{k}

with overwhelming probability for any
k

[
44
]
. This allows for,

\displaystyle z\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ii}(z)-\mathbb{E}[G_{ii}(z)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}(z)H_{ia}-\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)H_{ia}])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=2}^{5}\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{n-1}s_{n}}{N^{n/2}(n-1)!}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{n-1}e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}(z)-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{n-1}G_{ia}(z)])]

(10.6)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}((1+|\xi|)^{6}N^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1})^{6})

In the next few subsections we will deal with each of the terms on the second last line of (
10.1
). We note that many of the terms that appear when the derivatives
\partial_{ia}
act on
e_{\mathfrak{a}}
have been dealt with in Section
6.1
in the proof of Proposition
6.1
and so we will refer to the results in that section as appropriate. As in that proof, we will refer to the term in the second line of (
10.1
) with
n=k
as the
k
th order term.

Throughout the proof we will use the notation
z=x\pm\mathrm{i}y
as well as
w=u\pm\mathrm{i}v
and
w_{i}=u_{i}\pm\mathrm{i}v_{i}
. We will often use the fact that since
f
is assumed to be supported in
[-5,5]
that
\|f\|_{1}\leq C\|f^{\prime}\|_{1}\leq C^{2}\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}
to simplify some error terms. In applying the entrywise local law (
2.12
) we will often use that
\Psi(z)\leq C(Ny)^{-1/2}
when
y\geq N^{-1}
.

10.1.1
Second order term

Lemma 10.2
.

For any
\varepsilon>0
we have,

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})}{N}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}(z)-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}G_{ia}(z)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2Nm_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle-\frac{2\mathrm{i}\xi}{N}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\psi_{a}\partial_{w}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

(10.7)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1})y^{-1}+N^{-1}y^{-2}(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1})).

Proof.
We write,

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})}{N}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}(z)-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}G_{ia}(z)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-N\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(m_{N}(z)^{2}-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)^{2}])]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(\partial_{z}m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{z}m_{N}(z)])]

(10.8)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1+\delta_{ia}}{N}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{ia}e_{\mathfrak{a}})G_{ia}(z)]

Using the local law (
2.11
) we have,

\displaystyle-N\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(m_{N}(z)^{2}-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)^{2}])]

\displaystyle=-2Nm_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])]

(10.9)

\displaystyle+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-2}),

and

(10.10)

\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(\partial_{z}m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{z}m_{N}(z)])]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-2}).

The last term is,

\displaystyle\frac{1+\delta_{ia}}{N}\sum_{ia}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{ia}e_{\mathfrak{a}})G_{ia}(z)]

(10.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\mathrm{i}\xi}{N}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{w}\mathrm{tr}G(z)G(w)]\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v.

In order to deal with this term, we apply the estimate (
6.1.1
) derived in the proof of Lemma
6.2
. We first have, denoting
w=u+\mathrm{i}v
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}|(f(u)+\mathrm{i}f^{\prime}(u)v)\chi^{\prime}(v)|\left|\mathbb{E}\left[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\left(\partial_{w}\mathrm{tr}G(w)G(z)-\partial_{w}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}\right)\right]\right|\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}|(f(u)+\mathrm{i}f^{\prime}(u)v)\chi^{\prime}(v)|N^{-1}(y^{-2}v^{-1}+v^{-2}(y+v)^{-1})\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

(10.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CN^{\varepsilon}(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1})N^{-1}y^{-2}

Let
t=N^{\varepsilon}(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1})\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}^{-1}\wedge 1
. We have,

\displaystyle\int_{N^{\mathfrak{a}-1}<|v|<t}|f^{\prime\prime}(u)v\chi(v)|\left|\mathbb{E}\left[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\left(\partial_{w}\mathrm{tr}G(w)G(z)-\partial_{w}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}\right)\right]\right|\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

(10.13)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CN^{\varepsilon}\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}\int_{N^{-1}<v<t}vN^{-1}(y^{-2}v^{-1}+y^{-1}v^{-2})\mathrm{d}v\leq CN^{3\varepsilon-1}((1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1})y^{-2}+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}y^{-1}).

By integration by parts, the Cauchy-Riemann equations (i.e.,
\partial_{z}F=\partial_{x}F
for holomorphic
F
) and the Cauchy integral formula we have,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|v|>t}f^{\prime\prime}(u)v\chi(v)\mathbb{E}\left[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\left(\partial_{w}\mathrm{tr}G(w)G(z)-\partial_{w}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}\right)\right]\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|v|>t}f^{\prime}(u)v\chi(v)\mathbb{E}\left[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\left(\partial^{2}_{w}\mathrm{tr}G(w)G(z)-\partial^{2}_{w}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}\right)\right]\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\right|

(10.14)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CN^{\varepsilon}\|f^{\prime}\|_{1}\int_{t<v<2}N^{-1}v(y^{-2}v^{-2}+y^{-1}v^{-3})\mathrm{d}v\leq CN^{3\varepsilon-1}(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1})y^{-2}+(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1})y^{-1}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{2\mathrm{i}\xi}{N}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{w}\mathrm{tr}G(w)G(w)]\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\mathrm{i}\xi}{N}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\psi_{a}\partial_{w}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

(10.15)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{3\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}((1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1})N^{-1}y^{-2}+N^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1})y^{-1}).

This completes the proof. ∎

10.1.2
Third order term

Lemma 10.3
.

We have,

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}}{N^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}[\partial^{2}_{ia}e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}(z)-\mathbb{E}[\partial^{2}_{ia}G_{ia}(z)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}\frac{8m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)\mathrm{i}\xi}{\pi N^{1/2}}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)(m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)+m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z))m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{4\xi^{2}}{\pi^{2}N^{1/2}}\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}(1+|\xi|)\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}(\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}+1)y^{-1})+N^{\varepsilon}|\xi|^{2}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1/2}(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1})(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}))

(10.16)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-3/2})

Proof.
We write,

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}}{N^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}[\partial^{2}_{ia}e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}(z)-\mathbb{E}[\partial^{2}_{ia}G_{ia}(z)])]

(10.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}}{N^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(\partial_{ia}^{2}G_{ia}(z)-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{2}G_{ia}(z)])]

(10.18)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{2(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}}{N^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{ia}G_{ia}(z)]

(10.19)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}}{N^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}[(\partial^{2}_{ia}e_{\mathfrak{a}})G_{ia}(z)].

We start with (
10.17
). Using (
A.4
) this is split into,

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}}{N^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(\partial_{ia}^{2}G_{ia}(z)-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{2}G_{ia}(z)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sum_{ia}2\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}(z)^{3}-\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)^{3}])]

(10.20)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sum_{ia}6\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}(z)G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)-\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)])].

By similar logic as in the estimation of the term (
6.19
) we obtain,

(10.21)

\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sum_{ia}2\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}(z)^{3}-\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)^{3}])]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-3/2}).

Similar logic as in the estimation of the term (
6.20
) yields,

(10.22)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sum_{ia}6\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}(z)G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)-\mathbb{E}[G_{ia}(z)G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)])]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-3/2}).

This completes estimation of the term (
10.17
). We turn to (
10.18
). It is split into,
