\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{2(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}}{N^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{ia}G_{ia}(z)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}\xi\sum_{ia}\frac{4}{\pi N^{3/2}}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)(G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z))]

(10.23)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}\xi\sum_{ia}\frac{4}{\pi N^{3/2}}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)G_{ia}(z)^{2}]

Similar logic to the estimation of (
6.21
) yields that the following estimate holds with overwhelming probability,

(10.24)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sum_{ia}\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)=N^{-1/2}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)+N^{\varepsilon}(N^{-1}v^{-3/2}y^{-1}).

By direct integration, we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}\xi\sum_{ia}\frac{4}{N^{3/2}}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)(G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z))]

(10.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}\xi 4N^{-1/2}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}+N^{\varepsilon}|\xi|\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1})).

In a similar fashion, we use the logic used to estimate (
6.22
) to find,

\displaystyle i\xi\sum_{ia}\frac{4}{\pi N^{3/2}}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)G_{ia}(z)^{2}]

(10.26)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}\xi 4N^{-1/2}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}+N^{\varepsilon}|\xi|\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1})).

This completes the estimation of (
10.18
). We split the term (
10.19
) into,

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}}{N^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}[(\partial^{2}_{ia}e_{\mathfrak{a}})G_{ia}(z)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{2\mathrm{i}\xi}{\pi N^{3/2}}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{w}(G_{ii}(w)G_{aa}(w))G_{ia}(z)]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{2\mathrm{i}\xi}{\pi N^{3/2}}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{w}(G_{ia}(w)^{2})G_{ia}(z)]

(10.27)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{-4\xi^{2}}{\pi^{2}N^{3/2}}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{w}_{1}}\partial_{\bar{w}_{2}}\tilde{f}(w_{1})\tilde{f}(w_{2})\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{w_{1}}G_{ia}(w_{1})\partial_{w_{2}}G_{ia}(w_{2})G_{ia}(z)]

Similar logic to the estimation of (
6.23
) and (
6.24
) shows the estimates,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sum_{ia}G_{ia}(z)G_{ii}(w)\partial_{w}G_{aa}(w)

(10.28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1/2}v^{-2})

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sum_{ia}G_{ia}(z)G_{ia}(w)\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)

(10.29)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1/2}v^{-2})

holds with overwhelming probability. Therefore, by direct integration, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{2\mathrm{i}\xi}{\pi N^{3/2}}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{w}(G_{ia}(w)^{2}+G_{ii}(w)G_{aa}(w))G_{ia}(z)]

(10.30)

\displaystyle=\frac{8\mathrm{i}\xi}{\pi N^{1/2}}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}+N^{\varepsilon}|\xi|\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1})y^{-1/2}).

Denote
w_{i}=u_{i}\pm v_{i}
.
Appying the entry-wise local law (
2.12
) we see that with overwhelming probability,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sum_{ia}\partial_{w_{1}}G_{ia}(w_{1})\partial_{w_{2}}G_{ia}(w_{2})G_{ia}(z)

(10.31)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w_{1})m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w_{2})}{N^{1/2}}+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1/2}v_{1}^{-3/2}v_{2}^{-3/2}),

and so by a similar argument to that leading to the estimate (
10.1.1
) we have,

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{-4\xi^{2}}{\pi^{2}N^{3/2}}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{w}_{1}}\partial_{\bar{w}_{2}}\tilde{f}(w_{1})\tilde{f}(w_{2})\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{w_{1}}G_{ia}(w_{1})\partial_{w_{2}}G_{ia}(w_{2})G_{ia}(z)]

(10.32)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-4\xi^{2}}{\pi^{2}N^{1/2}}\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v\right)^{2}+N^{\varepsilon}|\xi|^{2}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1/2}(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1})(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}))

This yields the claim. ∎

10.1.3
Fourth order term

Lemma 10.4
.

We have,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{3}e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}(z)-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{3}G_{ia}(z)])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-12\xi\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

(10.33)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}(1+|\xi|^{3})(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1}^{3})\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}))

Proof.
We write,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{3}e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}(z)-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{3}G_{ia}(z)])]

(10.34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(\partial_{ia}^{3}G_{ia}(z)-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{3}G_{ia}(z)])]

(10.35)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{3(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{ia}e_{\mathfrak{a}})(\partial_{ia}^{2}G_{ia}(z))]

(10.36)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{3(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{ia}^{2}e_{\mathfrak{a}})(\partial_{ia}G_{ia}(z))]

(10.37)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{ia}^{3}e_{\mathfrak{a}})(G_{ia}(z))]

For (
10.34
), the same logic used to estimate (
6.42
) shows that with overwhelming probability,

(10.38)

\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{ia}(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}(\partial_{ia}^{3}G_{ia}(z)-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{3}G_{ia}(z)])=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1}).

For (
10.35
), we see from the entry-wise local law (
2.12
) that

(10.39)

\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{ia}\partial_{w}G_{ia}(w)(2G_{ia}^{3}(z)+6G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)G_{ia}(z))=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1/2}v^{-3/2})

holds with overwhelming probability. Therefore by direct integration,

(10.40)

\sum_{ia}\frac{3(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{ia}e_{\mathfrak{a}})(\partial_{ia}^{2}G_{ia}(z))]=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(|\xi|N^{-1}y^{-1/2}\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}).

For (
10.36
), we first write it as

\displaystyle\sum_{ia}\frac{(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{ia}^{2}e_{\mathfrak{a}})(\partial_{ia}G_{ia}(z))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-2\xi\mathrm{i}}{\pi N^{2}}\sum_{ia}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{w}(G_{ii}(w)G_{aa}(w)+G_{ia}(w)^{2})(G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)+G_{ia}(z)^{2})]

(10.41)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{4\xi^{2}}{\pi^{2}N^{2}}\sum_{ia}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{w}_{1}}\partial_{\bar{w}_{2}}\tilde{f}(w_{1})\tilde{f}(w_{2})\partial_{w_{1}}\partial_{w_{2}}\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}G_{ia}(w_{1})G_{ia}(w_{2})(G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)+G_{ia}(z)^{2})]

Using the same logic as leading to the estimate of (
6.1.3
), we see that with overwhelming probability,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{ia}\partial_{w}(G_{ii}(w)G_{aa}(w)+G_{ia}(w)^{2})(G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)+G_{ia}(z)^{2})

(10.42)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}+N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1}v^{-2}),

and so

\displaystyle\frac{-2\xi\mathrm{i}}{\pi N^{2}}\sum_{ia}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}\partial_{w}(G_{ii}(w)G_{aa}(w)+G_{ia}(w)^{2})(G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)+G_{ia}(z)^{2})]

(10.43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-4\xi\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)^{2}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v+N^{\varepsilon}|\xi|\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}y^{-1}).

By the entrywise local law (
2.12
) we have,

(10.44)

\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{ia}\partial_{w_{1}}\partial_{w_{2}}G_{ia}(w_{1})G_{ia}(w_{2})(G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)+G_{ia}(z)^{2})=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}v_{1}^{-3/2}v_{2}^{-3/2}),

with overwhelming probability. Therefore, by a similar argument as to the one that leads to (
10.1.1
) that uses integration by parts we find,

\displaystyle\frac{\xi^{2}}{\pi^{2}N^{2}}\sum_{ia}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{w}_{1}}\partial_{\bar{w}_{2}}\tilde{f}(w_{1})\tilde{f}(w_{2})\partial_{w_{1}}\partial_{w_{2}}\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}G_{ia}(w_{1})G_{ia}(w_{2})(G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)+G_{ia}(z)^{2})]

(10.45)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}|\xi|^{2}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1})(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1}))

This completes (
10.36
).
For the final term (
10.37
), we may simply use the estimate,

(10.46)

|\partial_{ia}^{3}e_{\mathfrak{a}}|\leq(1+|\xi|^{3})(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1}^{3})\left(\delta_{ia}+N^{-1/2}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1})^{1/2}\right)

which follows from direct calculation, the entry-wise local law (
2.12
) and an argument using integration by parts similar to the one leading to (
10.1.1
). This completes the proof.
∎

10.1.4
Fifth order term

Lemma 10.5
.

We have,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{5/2}}\sum_{ia}(1+\delta_{ia})^{4}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{4}(e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{4}G_{ia}])]

(10.47)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}(1+|\xi|^{4})(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1})^{4}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1}))

Proof.
We write the fifth order term as,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{5/2}}\sum_{ia}(1+\delta_{ia})^{4}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{4}(e_{\mathfrak{a}}(G_{ia}-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{4}G_{ia}])]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{5/2}}\sum_{ia}(1+\delta_{ia})^{4}\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(\partial_{ia}^{4}G_{ia}-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{4}G_{ia}])]

(10.48)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{4}\frac{1}{N^{5/2}}\sum_{ia}(1+\delta_{ia})^{4}c_{n}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{ia}^{n}e_{\mathfrak{a}})\partial_{ia}^{4-n}G_{ia}].

for some combinatorial factor
c_{n}
. From the entry-wise local law (
2.12
) we easily see that,

(10.49)

\frac{1}{N^{5/2}}\sum_{ia}(\partial_{ia}^{4}G_{ia}-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{ia}^{4}G_{ia}])=N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1/2})

with overwhelming probability. Consider now each of the terms in the last line of (
10.48
). Note that for
m=2k
even we have,

(10.50)

|\partial_{ia}^{2k}G_{ia}(z)|\leq C(\delta_{ia}+N^{\varepsilon}(Ny)^{-1/2})

with overwhelming probability. Together with the estimate (
10.5
) we see that the terms with
n=2,4
of the last line of (
10.48
) are
N^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}y^{-1/2}(1+|\xi|)^{4}(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1})^{4})
. For the terms with
n=1,3
note that the estimate (
10.46
) holds also with LHS
|\partial_{ia}e_{\mathfrak{a}}|
. Therefore, these terms are
N^{\varepsilon}(1+|\xi|^{3})(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1}^{3})\mathcal{O}(N^{-1/2}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1})^{1/2})
. This completes the proof. ∎

10.1.5
Proof of Proposition
10.1

This follows immediately from the lemmas of the previous subsections.

10.2
Proof of Theorem
3.2

The estimates for the expectation and variance are Lemma
6.11
and Theorem
6.9
, respectively. We turn to the esimate of the characteristic function.
We have

(10.51)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\xi}\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi)=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{f}(z)N\mathbb{E}[e_{\mathfrak{a}}(m_{N}(z)-\mathbb{E}[m_{N}(z)])]\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y.

From Proposition
10.1
we then see that,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\xi}\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=(-\xi V_{1}(f)+\frac{\mathrm{i}3\xi^{2}}{N^{1/2}}B_{1}(f))\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi)

(10.52)

\displaystyle+N^{\varepsilon}(1+|\xi|^{6})(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1,w}^{7})\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}))

where

(10.53)

B_{1}(f):=\frac{2}{3\pi^{3}}\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{f}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right)^{3}

and

(10.54)

V_{1}(f):=\int_{\Omega_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{f}(z)\partial_{\bar{w}}\tilde{f}(w)F(z,w)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v

where
F(z,w)
is defined by the function on the second and third lines of (
6.2
).
Now, with
V(f)
defined by (
6.9
) and
B(f)
defined by,

(10.55)

B(f):=\frac{2}{3\pi^{3}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{\bar{z}}\tilde{f}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\right)^{3}=-\frac{1}{12\pi^{3}}\left(\int_{-2}^{2}f(x)\frac{x}{\sqrt{4-x^{2}}}\mathrm{d}x\right)^{3},

it is not hard to derive (see the proof of Theorem
6.9
for the estimate of
V_{1}(f)-V(f)
),

(10.56)

|V_{1}(f)-V(f)|+|B_{1}(f)-B(f)|\leq N^{\varepsilon}(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1})^{2}N^{\mathfrak{a}-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\xi}\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=(-\xi V(f)+\frac{3\mathrm{i}\xi^{2}s_{3}}{N^{1/2}}B(f))\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi)

(10.57)

\displaystyle+N^{\mathfrak{a}+\varepsilon}(1+|\xi|^{6})(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1,w}^{7})\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}))

Therefore, using the fact that
B(f)
is real we have,

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi)-\exp\left[-\xi^{2}V(f)/2+\mathrm{i}\xi^{3}N^{-1/2}B(f)\right]\right|

(10.58)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CN^{\mathfrak{a}+\varepsilon}(1+|\xi|^{6})(1+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1,w}^{7})N^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w})\int_{0}^{\xi}\mathrm{e}^{(s^{2}-\xi^{2})V(f)}\mathrm{d}s.

By Lemma
E.1
we have that
V(f)\geq 0
for
N\geq 4
.
Therefore, the integral on the last line of (
10.2
) is bounded by
|\xi|
. Therefore, by Lemma
6.8
we have the following estimate,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[\exp(\mathrm{i}\xi(\mathrm{tr}f(H)-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{tr}f(H)]))]-\exp\left[-\xi^{2}V(f)/2+\mathrm{i}\xi^{3}N^{-1/2}B(f)\right]\right|

(10.59)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{\varepsilon}(1+|\xi|^{6}+\|f^{\prime}\|_{1,w}^{7})N^{-1}(1+\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{1,w}),

and the claim follows. ∎

Appendix A
Derivatives of resolvent entries with respect to matrix elements

In this section, we collect some identities for derivatives of resolvent entries with respect to matrix entries. We have the general formula

(A.1)

(1+\delta_{ia})\partial_{ia}G_{jk}=-G_{ij}G_{ka}-G_{aj}G_{ki}.

For first derivatives, this specializes to

(A.2)

(1+\delta_{ia})\partial_{ia}G_{ia}=-G_{ii}G_{aa}-G_{ia}^{2}

and

(A.3)

(1+\delta_{ia})\partial_{ia}G_{jj}=-2G_{ij}G_{ja}.

For second derivatives, we get

(A.4)

(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}\partial^{2}_{ia}(G_{ia})=2G_{ia}^{3}+6G_{ii}G_{aa}G_{ia}.

and

(A.5)

(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}(\partial_{ia}G_{ia}(z))(\partial_{ia}G_{jj}(w))=2G_{ii}(z)G_{aa}(z)G_{ji}(w)G_{ja}(w)+2G_{ia}(z)^{2}G_{ji}(w)G_{ja}(w)

and

(A.6)

(1+\delta_{ia})^{2}\partial^{2}_{ia}G_{jj}=4G_{ij}G_{ja}G_{ai}+2G_{ij}G_{ji}G_{aa}+2G_{aj}G_{ja}G_{ii}.

For the third derivatives, we have

(A.7)

\displaystyle-(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}\partial_{ia}^{3}G_{ia}

\displaystyle=6G_{ia}^{4}+36G_{ia}^{2}G_{ii}G_{aa}+6G_{ii}^{2}G_{aa}^{2},

as well as

(A.8)

\displaystyle-(1+\delta_{ia})^{3}G_{jj}=12G_{ai}^{2}G_{ij}G_{ja}+12G_{ja}^{2}G_{ii}G_{ai}+12G_{ij}^{2}G_{aa}G_{ai}+12G_{aj}G_{ij}G_{aa}G_{ii}

Appendix B
Semicircle calculations

Lemma B.1
.

We have,

(B.1)

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}=\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}.

Proof.
Recall,

(B.2)

m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)=\frac{-z+\sqrt{z^{2}-4}}{2},\qquad m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)+z=-\frac{1}{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}.

Consider,

(B.3)

\displaystyle m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-\frac{1}{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}

\displaystyle=m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)+w+m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)=\frac{1}{2}\left(-z+\sqrt{z^{2}-4}+w+\sqrt{w^{2}-4}\right)

(B.4)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(w-z+\frac{w^{2}-z^{2}}{\sqrt{w^{2}-4}-\sqrt{z^{2}-4}}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\left(w-z+\frac{(w-z)(w+z)}{\sqrt{w^{2}-4}-\sqrt{z^{2}-4}}\right)

(B.5)

\displaystyle=\frac{w-z}{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{w^{2}-4}-\sqrt{z^{2}-4}+w+z}{\sqrt{w^{2}-4}-\sqrt{z^{2}-4}}\right)

(B.6)

\displaystyle=(w-z)\left(\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)+w-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}{\sqrt{w^{2}-4}-\sqrt{z^{2}-4}}\right)

(B.7)

\displaystyle=(w-z)\left(\frac{-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-(m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w))^{-1}}{\sqrt{w^{2}-4}-\sqrt{z^{2}-4}}\right)

where in the second line we used difference of squares factoring
(\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b})(\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b})=a-b
. Hence,

\displaystyle 1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)

\displaystyle=-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)\left(m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-\frac{1}{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w))(w-z)\left(\frac{-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-(m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w))^{-1}}{\sqrt{w^{2}-4}-\sqrt{z^{2}-4}}\right)

(B.8)

\displaystyle=\frac{w-z}{\sqrt{w^{2}-4}-\sqrt{z^{2}-4}}(1+m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)).

Therefore:

(B.9)

\frac{\sqrt{w^{2}-4}-\sqrt{z^{2}-4}}{w-z}=\frac{1+m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}.

Continuing, we have:

(B.10)

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}

\displaystyle=\frac{-z+w+\sqrt{z^{2}-4}-\sqrt{w^{2}-4}}{2(z-w)}

(B.11)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\sqrt{w^{2}-4}-\sqrt{z^{2}-4}}{w-z}

(B.12)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1+m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}\right)

(B.13)

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}

This yields the claim. ∎

Lemma B.2
.

We have,

(B.14)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{z+2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}\partial_{w}\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)}{z-w}=-\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)}{(1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w))^{2}}

Proof.
This follows from the previous lemma and the fact that,

(B.15)

\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}{z+2m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)}=-m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z),

which follows from differentiating the quadratic equation satisfied by
m_{\mathrm{sc}}
. ∎

Lemma B.3
.

We have, for
z=x+\pm\mathrm{i}y
and
w=u\pm\mathrm{i}v
, where
v,y>0

(B.16)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)}{(1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w))^{2}}\right|\leq C\frac{|m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}^{\prime}(w)|}{(x-u)^{2}+y^{2}+v^{2}}

Proof.
From the fact that
|m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)|\leq 1-cy
we see that

(B.17)

|1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)|\geq c(y+v).

From (
B.1
) we see that

(B.18)

|1-m_{\mathrm{sc}}(z)m_{\mathrm{sc}}(w)|\geq c|z-w|\geq c|x-u|.

This completes the proof. ∎

Appendix C
Hermite polynomial asymptotics

We collect here some standard results on the Hermite polynomials
h_{n}(x)
, all available from
[
20
]
. In the bulk we have that there exists a
\delta_{a}>0
, we have

(C.1)

h_{n}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}x\right)\mathrm{e}^{-n\frac{x^{2}}{4}}=2\sqrt{\frac{1}{\pi\sqrt{2n}}}\frac{1}{(4-x^{2})^{1/4}}\left(\cos\left[\frac{n}{2}F(x)-\frac{1}{2}\arcsin\left(\frac{x}{2}\right)\right]+\mathcal{O}(n^{-1})\right)

uniformly in
-2+\delta_{a}\leq x\leq 2-\delta_{a}
where

(C.2)

F(x)=\left|\int_{x}^{2}\sqrt{|4-y^{2}|}\mathrm{d}y\right|.

In particular,

(C.3)

n^{1/4}\left|h_{n}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}x\right)\mathrm{e}^{-n\frac{x^{2}}{4}}\right|\leq C

for
|x|\leq 2-\delta_{a}
. Inside the spectrum, but near the edge we have

\displaystyle h_{n}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}x\right)\mathrm{e}^{-n\frac{x^{2}}{4}}

\displaystyle=(2n)^{-1/4}

\displaystyle\times\bigg\{\left(\frac{x+2}{x-2}\right)^{1/4}\left[\frac{3}{4}nF(x)\right]^{1/6}\mathrm{Ai}\left(-\left[\frac{3}{4}nF(x)\right]^{2/3}\right)(1+\mathcal{O}(n^{-1}))

(C.4)

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{x-2}{x+2}\right)^{1/4}\left[\frac{3}{4}nF(x)\right]^{-1/6}\mathrm{Ai}^{\prime}\left(-\left[\frac{3}{4}nF(x)\right]^{2/3}\right)(1+\mathcal{O}(n^{-1}))\bigg\},

for
2-\delta_{a}<x<2
.
Above,
\mathrm{Ai}
is the Airy function. It is bounded on the real line and smooth. So for
r>1
, the following asymptotics are useful, and can be found in Chapter 11, Section 1 of
[
49
]
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ai}(-r)

\displaystyle=\pi^{-1/2}r^{-1/4}\left\{\cos\left[\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right]+\mathcal{O}(r^{-3/2})\right\}

(C.5)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ai}^{\prime}(-r)

\displaystyle=\pi^{-1/2}r^{1/4}\left\{\sin\left[\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4}\right]+\mathcal{O}(r^{-3/2})\right\}.

Letting
x=2-y
for
\delta_{a}>y>0
we see that

(C.6)

nF(x)\asymp ny^{3/2}.

So, using the above we see that

(C.7)

\left|h_{n}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}x\right)\mathrm{e}^{-n\frac{x^{2}}{4}}\right|\leq\frac{C}{y^{1/4}+n^{-1/6}}

for
x=2-y
,
0<y<\delta_{a}
.

Now, near the edge, outside the spectrum we have, for
2<x<2+\delta_{a}
,

\displaystyle h_{n}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}x\right)\mathrm{e}^{-n\frac{x^{2}}{4}}

\displaystyle=(2n)^{-1/4}

\displaystyle\times\bigg\{\left(\frac{x+2}{x-2}\right)^{1/4}\left[\frac{3}{4}nF(x)\right]^{1/6}\mathrm{Ai}\left(\left[\frac{3}{4}nF(x)\right]^{2/3}\right)(1+\mathcal{O}(n^{-1}))

(C.8)

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{x-2}{x+2}\right)^{1/4}\left[\frac{3}{4}nF(x)\right]^{-1/6}\mathrm{Ai}^{\prime}\left(\left[\frac{3}{4}nF(x)\right]^{2/3}\right)(1+\mathcal{O}(n^{-1}))\bigg\}.

In this region, we have from
[
49
]
that,

(C.9)

|\mathrm{Ai}(r)|\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}}}{r^{1/4}},\qquad|\mathrm{Ai}^{\prime}(r)|\leq C(1+r)^{1/4}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}}.

Therefore, for
x=2+y
and
0<y<\delta_{a}
we have

(C.10)

\left|h_{n}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}x\right)\mathrm{e}^{-n\frac{x^{2}}{4}}\right|\leq\frac{C\mathrm{e}^{-cny^{3/2}}}{y^{1/4}+n^{-1/6}}

The asymptotics in
[
20
]
show that the above also extends to
y>\delta_{a}
.

It is a straightforward calculation to see that the above upper bounds extend also to

(C.11)

y\to\left|n^{1/4}h_{n-1}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}(2+y)\right)\mathrm{e}^{-n\frac{(2+y)^{2}}{4}}\right|.

Appendix D
Some integrals of Hermite polynomials

Let
H_{n}(x)
denote the physicist’s Hermite polynomials,

(D.1)

H_{n}(x)=(-1)^{n}\mathrm{e}^{x^{2}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\right)^{n}\mathrm{e}^{-x^{2}},

and the Hermite functions,

(D.2)

\varphi_{n}(x)=\frac{1}{(2^{n}n!\sqrt{\pi})^{1/2}}\mathrm{e}^{-x^{2}/2}H_{n}(x).

In this appendix we will use several results from Gradshteyn and Ryzhik
[
34
]
. There are many editions of this work, and all references are to the most recent 2014 version (8th edition) of
[
34
]
unless noted.

Lemma D.1
.

We have

(D.3)

\int\varphi_{2m}(x)\mathrm{d}x=\frac{2^{1/2}}{m^{1/4}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1})\right)

Proof.
The integral equals,

(D.4)

\int\varphi_{2m}(t)\mathrm{d}t=\frac{1}{2^{m}((2m)!)^{1/2}\pi^{1/4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{e}^{-t^{2}/2}H_{2m}(t)\mathrm{d}t.

Formula (7.376.1) of
[
34
]
is,

(D.5)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}xy}\mathrm{e}^{-x^{2}/2}H_{n}(x)\mathrm{d}x=(2\pi)^{1/2}H_{n}(y)\mathrm{e}^{-y^{2}/2}\mathrm{i}^{n}.

We need to evaluate this at
n=2m
and
y=0
.
Formula (8.956.6) of
[
34
]
is,

(D.6)

H_{2m}(0)=(-1)^{m}2^{m}(2m-1)!!

For the double factorial we have,

(D.7)

(2m-1)!!=\frac{(2m-1)!}{2^{m-1}(m-1)!}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi_{2m}(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2^{m}((2m)!)^{1/2}\pi^{1/4}}\right)\left((2\pi)^{1/2}(-1)^{m}\right)\left((-1)^{m}2^{m}\frac{(2m-1)!}{2^{m-1}(m-1)!}\right)

\displaystyle=2^{1/2}\pi^{1/4}\frac{(2m-1)!}{2^{m-1}((2m)!)^{1/2}(m-1)!}

(D.8)

\displaystyle=2^{1/2}\pi^{1/4}\frac{((2m)!)^{1/2}}{2^{m}(m)!}.

The first line is just plugging in the formulas (
D.5
), (
D.6
) and (
D.7
). The second line is just a few cancellations. In the third line we simplified the factorial terms. Stirling’s approximation is,

(D.9)

n!=(2\pi n)^{1/2}n^{n}\mathrm{e}^{-n}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(n^{-1})\right)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi_{2m}(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=2^{1/2}\pi^{1/4}\frac{((2m)!)^{1/2}}{2^{m}(m)!}

\displaystyle=2^{1/2}\pi^{1/4}\frac{(2\pi)^{1/4}(2m)^{1/4}(2m)^{m}\mathrm{e}^{-m}}{2^{m}(2\pi)^{1/2}m^{1/2}m^{m}\mathrm{e}^{-m}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1})\right)

(D.10)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{1/2}}{m^{1/4}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1})\right)

This yields the claim. ∎

Lemma D.2
.

We have,

(D.11)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2m+1}{2}\right)^{1/2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi_{2m+1}(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{m^{1/4}}{2^{1/2}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1/2})\right)

Proof.
Clearly
(\frac{2m+1}{2})^{1/2}=m^{1/2}(1+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1}))
. By definition,

(D.12)

\displaystyle m^{1/2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi_{2m+1}(t)\mathrm{d}t=

\displaystyle=\frac{m^{1/2}}{(2^{2m+1})^{1/2}((2m+1)!)^{1/2}\pi^{1/4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-t^{2}/2}H_{2m+1}(t)\mathrm{d}t.

We require the formula,

(D.13)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-2\alpha x^{2}}x^{\nu}H_{2n+1}(x)\mathrm{d}x=(-1)^{n}2^{2n-\nu/2}\frac{\Gamma(\nu/2+1)\Gamma(n+3/2)}{\pi^{1/2}\alpha^{\nu/2+1}}F(-n,\nu/2+1;3/2;(2\alpha)^{-1}),

where
F
is the (Gauss) hypergeometric function (also denoted
{}_{2}F_{1}
). It is given in the 2007 version (7th edition) of Gradshteyn and Ryzhik
[
33
]
as formula (7.376.3). It was then erroneously changed in the 2014 version
[
34
]
, and then corrected back to the 2007 (7th) edition formula in the erratum
[
56
]
1
.
†
†
footnotetext:
1
. The source of confusion seems to be that the original source
[
14
]
itself contains an error; note that the formula (13.18b) in that work is derived via substitution of (13.3b) into (10.4b). The
\mu
in (10.4b) equals
\mu=1/2
and so the
\Gamma(n+1/2)
in (13.18b) should be
\Gamma(n+\mu+1/2)=\Gamma(n+3/2)
. In any case, it is also not hard to directly check (
D.13
) from the relation
H_{2n+1}(x)=(-1)^{n}(2n+1)!(n!)^{-1}(2x)_{1}F_{1}(-n,3/2;x^{2})
, where
{}_{1}F_{1}
is the confluent hypergeometric function of the first kind, and direct integration of the series term-by-term.

Evaluating this with
n=m
and
\nu=0
and
\alpha=1/4
we see that,

\displaystyle\frac{m^{1/2}}{(2^{2m+1})^{1/2}((2m+1)!)^{1/2}\pi^{1/4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-t^{2}/2}H_{2m+1}(t)\mathrm{d}t

(D.14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m^{1/2}}{(2^{2m+1})^{1/2}((2m+1)!)^{1/2}\pi^{1/4}}(-1)^{m}2^{2m}\frac{\Gamma(m+3/2)}{\pi^{1/2}4^{-1}}F(-m,1;3/2;2),

where we simplified
\Gamma(1)=1
. From (8.339) of
[
34
]
and (
D.7
),

(D.15)

\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+n\right)=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2^{n}}(2n-1)!!=\frac{(2n)!}{4^{n}n!}\sqrt{\pi}.

Evaluating this with
n=m+1
we get,

(D.16)

\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{2}+m\right)=\frac{(2(m+1))!}{4^{m+1}(m+1)!}\sqrt{\pi}.

We use the following integral representation of the hypergeometric function, (9.111) of
[
34
]

(D.17)

F(a,b;c;z)=\frac{1}{B(b,c-b)}\int_{0}^{1}t^{b-1}(1-t)^{c-b-1}(1-tz)^{-a}\mathrm{d}t,

where the beta function is,

(D.18)

B(x,y)=\frac{\Gamma(x)\Gamma(y)}{\Gamma(x+y)}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle F(-m,1,3/2,2)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(3/2)}{\Gamma(1)\Gamma(1/2)}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{-1/2}(1-2t)^{m}\mathrm{d}t

(D.19)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{-1/2}(1-2t)^{m}\mathrm{d}t

So,

\displaystyle\frac{m^{1/2}}{(2^{2m+1})^{1/2}((2m+1)!)^{1/2}\pi^{1/4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-t^{2}/2}H_{2m+1}(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{m^{1/2}}{(2^{2m+1})^{1/2}((2m+1)!)^{1/2}\pi^{1/4}}\right)\times\left(\frac{(-1)^{m}2^{2m}}{\pi^{1/2}4^{-1}}\frac{(2(m+1))!}{4^{m+1}(m+1)!}\sqrt{\pi}\right)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{-1/2}(1-2t)^{m}\mathrm{d}t\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{-1/4}(-1)^{m}\frac{m^{1/2}(2(m+1))!}{2^{m}2^{1/2}(m+1)!((2m+1)!)^{1/2}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{-1/2}(1-2t)^{m}\mathrm{d}t\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{-1/4}\frac{m^{1/2}(2m+2)(2m+1)((2m)!)^{1/2}}{2^{m}2^{3/2}(m+1)m!(2m+1)^{1/2}}\left((-1)^{m}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{-1/2}(1-2t)^{m}\mathrm{d}t\right)

(D.20)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{-1/4}m\left(\frac{((2m)!)^{1/2}}{2^{m}m!}\right)\left((-1)^{m}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{-1/2}(1-2t)^{m}\mathrm{d}t\right)\left(1+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1})\right)

The first equality is direct substitution of (
D.16
) and (
D
) into (
D
). In the second equality we cleaned up the powers of
2
and
\pi
. In the third equality we simplified the factorials to only have
(2m)!
and
m!
. The third asymptotic is using things like
2m+1\sim 2m
, etc., and simplifying the powers of
2
. Via Stirling’s approximation,

(D.21)

\frac{((2m)!)^{1/2}}{2^{m}m!}=\frac{(2\pi)^{1/4}(2m)^{1/4}(2m)^{m}\mathrm{e}^{-m}}{2^{m}(2\pi)^{1/2}m^{1/2}m^{m}\mathrm{e}^{-m}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1})\right)=\pi^{-1/4}m^{-1/4}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1})\right).

Now consider the integral,

(D.22)

(-1)^{m}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{-1/2}(1-2t)^{m}\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{1}(2t-1)^{m}(1-t)^{-1/2}\mathrm{d}t.

It is easy to see that,

(D.23)

\int_{0}^{1/2}|1-2t|^{m}(1-t)^{-1/2}\leq 2\int_{0}^{1/2}(1-2t)^{m}\leq\frac{C}{m}

by exact calculation. For the other half of the integral,

\displaystyle\int_{1/2}^{1}(2t-1)^{m}(1-t)^{-1/2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1/2}(1-2u)^{m}u^{-1/2}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1/2}\mathrm{e}^{m\log(1-2u)}u^{-1/2}\mathrm{d}u

(D.24)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{1/2}m}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\left[-x(1+m^{-1})\right]\frac{1}{(1-\mathrm{e}^{-x/m})^{1/2}}\mathrm{d}x,

via the change of variable
x=-m\log(1-2u)
.
It is straightforward to check that,

(D.25)

\left|\frac{1}{(1-\mathrm{e}^{-s})^{1/2}}-\frac{1}{s^{1/2}}\right|\leq C

for all
s>0
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{1/2}m}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\left[-x(1+m^{-1})\right]\frac{1}{(1-\mathrm{e}^{-x/m})^{1/2}}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{1/2}m^{1/2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\left[-x(1+m^{-1})\right]x^{-1/2}\mathrm{d}x+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1})

(D.26)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{1/2}m^{1/2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\left[-x\right]x^{-1/2}\mathrm{d}x+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1})

The final integral can be calculated exactly by relating it to the Gaussian integral,

(D.27)

\frac{1}{2^{1/2}m^{1/2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\left[-x\right]x^{-1/2}\mathrm{d}x=\frac{\pi^{1/2}}{2^{1/2}m^{1/2}}.

Summarizing,

(D.28)

(-1)^{m}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{-1/2}(1-2t)^{m}\mathrm{d}t=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{\sqrt{2m}}(1+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1/2})).

Plugging (
D.21
) and (
D.28
) into (
D.20
) we obtain,

\displaystyle\frac{m^{1/2}}{(2^{2m+1})^{1/2}((2m+1)!)^{1/2}\pi^{1/4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-t^{2}/2}H_{2m+1}(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{-1/4}m\left(\pi^{-1/4}m^{-1/4}\right)(\pi^{1/2}(2m)^{-1/2})\left(1+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1/2})\right)

(D.29)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m^{1/4}}{2^{1/2}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(m^{-1/2})\right).

This completes the proof. ∎

Appendix E
Verification that the variance functional is positive

Lemma E.1
.

The functional
V(\varphi)
defined in (
3
) is positive for
N\geq 4
.

Proof.
With the functional
V(\varphi)
as in (
3
) we have, (see, e.g.,
[
40
, Theorem 2.4]
)

(E.1)

V(\varphi)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}kc_{k}^{2}+\frac{s_{4}}{2}c_{2}^{2}-\frac{2s_{3}}{N^{1/2}}c_{1}c_{2}

where
c_{i}
is the
i
th coefficient of
\varphi
in the Chebyshev polynomial basis,

(E.2)

c_{k}=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\varphi(2\cos(\theta))\cos(k\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta=\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi(2x)T_{k}(x)\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{\sqrt{1-x^{2}}}

and
T_{k}
is the
k
th Chebyschev polynomial of the first kind,
T_{k}(\cos(\theta))=\cos(k\theta)
.

Now for any centered random variable with variance
1
we have the inequality, (see, for example, the remark after
[
29
, Theorem 2.2]
)

(E.3)

s_{4}\geq s_{3}^{2}-2.

Hence,

\displaystyle V(\varphi)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{2}+c_{2}^{2}+\frac{s_{4}}{2}c_{2}^{2}-2\frac{s_{3}}{N^{1/2}}c_{1}c_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{2}+\frac{s_{3}^{2}}{2}c_{2}^{2}-2\frac{s_{3}}{N^{1/2}}c_{1}c_{2}

(E.4)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{2}+\frac{s_{3}^{2}}{2}c_{2}^{2}-\frac{s_{3}^{2}}{2}c_{2}^{2}-2\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{N}.

We therefore conclude the proof. ∎
